Either present it that way (a treasury/opportunity-cost argument with an explicit horizon and a stated view on the stock's expected return) or drop the comparison and size the buyback off the capital plan and leverage capacity. **Fix:** Rewrite slide 4's title and the bottom callout to remove the 4.12% T-bill comparison as the sizing rationale. Replace with the capital-plan logic: 'Fund the $7.5B plan, hold the $3.968B minimum buffer, return the residual: $3.0B buyback, not $4.0B.' If the T-bill comparison is retained, move it to a footnote and label it as an opportunity-cost observation, not a value test.
- $250M/month × 12 months = $3.0B exactly, so the arithmetic ties, but the deck gives no source for the $250M monthly pace and no bridge showing it is achievable against average daily volume or the Jan-2027 authorization timing. 'About' is doing the work; a committee will ask why this cadence and not $375M/quarter or front-loaded. Also item 4 says 'Three months of operating cash flow is $3.968B' — that is a quarterly figure from the 10-Q, but nothing on slide 5 states which quarter or that it is being used as a proxy for the minimum buffer; the reader must reconstruct it from slide 3 footnote 1. **Fix:** On slide 5 item 2, replace '$250M a month' with the derived figure and its basis, e.g. '$3.0B / 12 months = $250M per month; assumes even execution from the January 2027 renewal.' Add a source line to the $250M this-cell or an explicit note that it is the deck's pace assumption, not company guidance (footnote 3 currently covers buyback size and special dividend, not the pace). On item 4, write 'Three months of fiscal 2026 operating cash flow is $3.968B (10-Q for period ended 2026-05-10)' and state explicitly that this is the deck's proxy for the minimum operating cash buffer.
- The single largest number in the deck, $7.5B FY2027 capex, is an unconfirmed re-typed figure from a secondary site. Every downstream claim ($15.82B covers it 2.1x, $6B is surplus, $22.53B closing cash) rests on it. Slide 3 even shows FY2026 capex of $6.43B; nothing in the material explains a $1.07B / 16.6% step-up or what it buys (warehouse count, land, automation). **Fix:** Pull the FY2026 10-K Item 7 and the FY2026 capex disclosure; replace 're-typing' with a primary citation or replace $7.5B with the disclosed figure. If $7.5B is management's plan rather than a filing, label it 'Management FY2027 plan' and add a one-line bridge from $6.43B FY2026 actual to $7.5B with the driver (new warehouses, land, remodels).
- Item 1 of slide 2 implies the buyback returns $4B ('Every $1B retires 0.90M shares'), item 3 says it is only a ceiling, and footnote 1 concedes 'the FY2027 repurchase amount is not in the materials and is a research request.' So the 'cash walk' on slide 2 treats the full $4B as an outflow while the body text treats it as a ceiling. The deck simultaneously assumes $4B is returned and not returned. **Fix:** Pick one treatment. Either show the cash walk with the actual assumed repurchase (state the number and its source or mark it as management estimate) or show the walk to 'surplus available for return' and present the $4B as the authorization ceiling. Rewrite item 1 of slide 2 to match whatever the walk shows.
- The arithmetic does not hold. $15.82B less $7.5B is $8.32B. The deck never bridges the $2.32B gap between the $8.32B residual and the $6B return capacity. Slide 2 also states "The 22.2% return on capital is the existing book", a capex denominator the $6B ignores. **Fix:** Insert an explicit bridge on slide 2 (or a new back-up page): opening cash $20.21B + operating cash flow $15.82B − capex $7.5B − other utilizations (define each: dividends, debt, working capital, taxes) = surplus $6B, and tie the $6B to the closing cash of $21.38B. If the $2.32B is the regular dividend, say so and quantify it.
- The chart's own claim of "12.6% a year over three years" is a CAGR that requires FY2023 and FY2026; with FY2024 and FY2025 absent, the growth statement is unsupported on the page and the reader cannot verify it. A board will ask for the series. **Fix:** Add the FY2023–FY2026 operating cash flow series to the chart with the source for each year, or delete the 12.6% claim and replace it with a statement the page can evidence (e.g. FY2025 → FY2026 change, sourced).
- $1B ÷ $1,106.21 = 904,000 shares, not 0.90M (which is 900,000). The implied price per share behind the share count is not consistent with the stated price. This also matters because slide 5 sets the buyback floor at 25x $20.76 EPS ≈ $519 a share, roughly half the $1,106.21 average cost — a 53% gap the deck never reconciles. **Fix:** Use 0.904M shares per $1B (or restate the per-share price consistently), and add a line reconciling the $1,106.21 average cost with the ~$519 floor: state what period the average cost is measured over and why the floor is set well below it.
- The $8.32B surplus is asserted with no derivation on the page, and the $2.32B residual is hand-waved to "the regular dividend run-rate and other uses" without a number. $6B + $2.32B = $8.32B arithmetically, but nothing shows what the regular dividend run-rate actually costs or how much of the $2.32B it consumes. This is the page the board will do arithmetic on. **Fix:** Add a source line to slide 2 for the $8.32B (tie it to the cash walk: $20.21B opening + $15.82B operating cash flow less the working-capital, regular dividend and other uses that produce $8.32B), and replace "and other uses" with the actual regular dividend run-rate figure in $B so the $2.32B residual is fully allocated. If the number is not in the model, say "$2.32B retained, of which $XB regular dividend run-rate" and cite the K-10.
- The page compares three uses of $1B on a so-called "one after-tax return basis" but the metrics are not comparable: warehouse capex is a 22.2% historical average return on an existing $42.32B invested capital base, buyback is a 1.88% EPS-accretion test (not a return), and the special dividend is $2.26 per share (a payout, no return at all). Slide 4's own side panel admits the 22.2% is "historical company average on the existing $42.32B invested capital" and "reference only, not the decision metric" — so the headline comparison is a false equivalence presented under a single "return basis" title. **Fix:** Rename slide 4's title to "Three uses of $1B: historical returns, EPS accretion and payout per share" and relabel the column "After-tax return / accretion per $1B" with each row stating its own metric (warehouse: historical ROIC on existing capital; buyback: FY2026 EPS accretion; dividend: cash per share). Or drop the dividend row from the comparison and present it separately.
- The waterfall does not foot. Opening 20.21 + 15.82 - 6.43 - 13.29 - 2.61 - 0.85 = 12.85 exactly, so the bars are arithmetically consistent, but the bridge omits any interest income on the cash balance, and it presents $12.85B as if it is the year-end cash — that figure is pre-anything on the ~$13.3B dividend that is actually declared on 11 Nov and payable 4 Dec. If the dividend is paid in the fiscal year, the closing cash cannot be 12.85 unless the opening cash and the operating cash flow are being double-counted. State the definition of 'leaves $12.85B' — is it post-dividend, pre-dividend, or an intra-year trough? As written a board member will add the column and ask the same question. **Fix:** Rebuild slide 4 with an explicit sub-total: 'Cash after special dividend and buyback before operating cash flow' and 'Cash after operating cash flow', and label the closing bar 'Cash 31 Aug 2027 (post-dividend, post-buyback, pre-other financing)'. If the $12.85B is pre-dividend, correct it; the post-dividend figure should be 20.21 + 15.82 - 6.43 - 13.29 - 2.61 - 0.85 = 12.85 only if the dividend is counted once. Show the interest income line (>$0.5B on $20B at 4%+) or state explicitly that interest income is excluded and why.
- Three different share-count / dividend figures are used without reconciliation: slide 2 says '$13.29B ... 443.1MM shares', slide 5 says '$13.29B is the $30.00 a share declared on 443.1MM shares outstanding at 2026-09-29; the company states approximately $13.38'. $30.00 × 443.1MM = $13.293B, so the arithmetic holds, but slide 2's headline says '$13.3B' and slide 4's waterfall uses $13.29B while its title says '$13.29B covers'. Pick $13.29B everywhere and footnote the $13.38B company statement with the share-count assumption (which shares count — record date 24 Nov, ex-date). **Fix:** Standardise on $13.29B ($30.00 × 443.1MM) on slides 2, 4 and 5, and on slide 2 replace '$13.3B' in the title with '$13.29B'. Add to slides 2 and 4 footnote: 'Company states ~$13.38B; difference is 443.1MM vs. an assumed ~446.0MM shares at record date 24 Nov 2026.'
- The bridge does not foot. 20.21 + 15.82 - 6.43 - 13.29 - 2.61 - 0.85 = 12.85, but the printed closing balance is 12.85 and the stated uses exclude the $4B buyback authorization draw and any working capital: the sum of the visible bars implies a $7.71B net movement, yet the page claims $12.85B closing with no reconciling item. A reader will foot this in ten seconds and stop reading. **Fix:** Re-foot the waterfall and show every reconciling line: 20.21 + 15.82 operating - 6.43 capex - 13.29 special - 2.61 regular - 0.85 buyback = X. If X is not 12.85, either correct the bars or add the missing line (e.g. other investing/financing) with a source. If the $4B authorization is a ceiling, say so in the bar label and keep it out of the arithmetic.
- Operating cash flow of $15.82B has no source, no period and no tie to slide 2's '$9.38B free cash flow, fiscal 2026'. Two different operating cash figures on adjacent pages (15.82 vs 9.38) with no bridge between them is exactly what a committee catches. **Fix:** State the period and source for the 15.82 figure in the footnote (e.g. 'FY2026 operating cash flow, 10-K'), and add a one-line reconciliation from 15.82 operating to 9.38 free cash flow (-6.43 capex = 9.39, rounding).
- Decision block on slide 2: 'approve a $1B-a-year buyback pace under the $4B authorization' — the $4B is a share repurchase authorization ceiling, not a 1-year capacity commitment, and the buyback is proposed as subject to a growth hurdle. The recommendation conflates a ceiling with an annual pace and asks the board to approve a mechanism that may not draw (slide 4: 'Buyback pace is justified only if three-year operating cash flow growth resumes above 4.52%'). **Fix:** Split the ask: 'Approve a $1B-a-year repurchase pace within the $4B authorization, first tranche in Q1 2027, suspended if three-year operating cash flow growth falls below 4.52% as tested on slide 4.' Show the hurdle in the recommendation and the table owner/timing as 'Treasurer, after break-even schedule'.
- Slide 3 calls the $12B an 'operating floor' and a 'proposed liquidity policy', yet footnote 1 says it is management's proposed liquidity policy at about 4% of FY26 revenue ($303.15B), with no company-stated minimum and no peer benchmark. The $12.99B 'cash trough' therefore cannot be tested against a peer or company standard, and the cushion ($0.99B / $0.01B) is an artefact of an unsourced assumption. **Fix:** Show the $12B as 4% of FY26 revenue with a sensitivity grid of trough vs. floor at $8B, $12B, $16B, and add a peer benchmark table of liquidity policy (cash + revolver availability) or state explicitly that no peer benchmark exists and the board is being asked to set one. If no peer data exists, request it.
- The free cash flow figure does not reconcile. Slide 3's bridge has operating cash flow 15.82, capex -6.43, regular dividend -2.46, buybacks -0.85, special dividend -13.30; 15.82 - 6.43 = 9.39 of FCF, and after 2.46 + 0.85 = 3.31 of ordinary capital returns the trough is 20.21 + 9.39 - 2.46 - 0.85 - 13.30 = 12.99, which ties, but 9.39 is not 9.38 and the label 'free cash flow' is used on slide 2 as if it were the same 9.38 number. A client will add this in the room. **Fix:** Set free cash flow = operating cash flow less capex = 15.82 - 6.43 = 9.39 on slide 2 (and footnote 1) and confirm every downstream reference uses 9.39; if 9.38 is the model number, show the model cell and the 0.01 of rounding explicitly.
- The base case of 12.6% three-year operating cash flow growth is not sourced to anything on the page. The only external figure cited is the consensus operating cash flow of about $14.0B, which implies a decline (the right column says -12%). We cannot put a 12.6% growth case in the book without a named source — company guidance, a broker consensus, or our own model with the assumptions listed. **Fix:** Either replace 12.6% with a sourced base case (state the source and the growth rate it implies for FY26-FY29) or move 12.6% to a clearly labelled 'management plan / illustrative' case and add a third scenario row at the consensus growth rate; the break-even and pause test should be shown against the same scenarios on slide 5.
- The cell reads 'N/A — Needs the capex fact confirmed', while capex of $6.43B is already used on slides 2 and 3. A board book cannot contain a live 'N/A', and the inconsistency signals the underlying fact is not controlled. **Fix:** Replace the 'N/A' cell with a real, sourced metric (e.g. capex as % of net income = $6.43B / $9.23B = 69.7%, matching slide 3) or delete the cell and rebalance the grid to three tiles. Do not ship any placeholder text.
- The deck recommends 'a sized special dividend' but never sizes it. Slide 2 concedes 'the size is not yet set'; slide 5 asks the board to 'size the dividend against a buyback' after further work. There is no dollar amount, no per-share amount, no bridge from $20.21B cash to distributable surplus, and no comparison of dividend vs buyback accretion. **Fix:** Add the arithmetic: reserve = minimum cash (state it) + capex and liquidity needs; distributable surplus = $20.21B less reserve; per-share = surplus / 443.1M shares. Show the same surplus deployed as a buyback at a stated price and compare EPS accretion. If the inputs don't exist, remove the recommendation from slide 2 and retitle this as a diagnostic deck.
- The core claim 'Growth is self-funded by earnings' rests on net income $9.23B covering capex $6.43B '1.4 times', but self-funding is an operating cash flow question. Slide 5 step 1 admits 'Get operating cash flow' — i.e. the deck does not have the cash flow number that actually tests the claim. Net income also excludes working-capital and inventory build, which for a warehouse rollout is the real cash drag. **Fix:** Pull operating cash flow from the 10-K cash flow statement and restate point 1 as OCF less capex. If OCF is not available, cut the word 'self-funded' and say 'capex covered 1.4x by net income (cash flow not yet confirmed)'.
- A capital-allocation recommendation with no valuation support: no DCF, no comps, no football field, no ROIC on new warehouses versus the cost of capital, no cost of equity or WACC. There is nothing on these pages that justifies a special dividend over reinvestment — the claimed decision hinges on 'the balance sheet has room', which is not an economics argument. **Fix:** Either add the valuation work — incremental ROIC on new warehouse capex vs WACC, and a payout-vs-reinvest comparison — or retitle slide 2 from a recommendation to 'Findings to date and the analysis still required before a payout decision'.
- "Earnings of $9.23B cover $6.43B of capital spending about 1.4 times over" — 9.23/6.43 = 1.44 and 9.23-6.43 = $2.80B, so the title's ratio is inconsistent with the $2.80B quoted directly beneath it; and the comparison is net income against capex, not cash flow against capex, which is what the chart header "Cash generation and earnings both exceed capex" implies. Also "1.4 times over" and "1.4x" mix ratio and coverage language. **Fix:** State one measure and one number: title as "Fiscal 2026 net income of $9.23B was 1.4x capex of $6.43B, leaving $2.80B" and in the first bullet write "Net income of $9.23B less capex of $6.43B leaves $2.80B", deleting "not cash-constrained" (slide 2 point 2 concedes the required reserve and warehouse returns are not yet established).
- The deck asks the board to approve sizing work today but never sizes anything. Slide 2 item 3 says 'Payout size and returns are not yet shown' and slide 5 step 3 defers sizing to 'CFO, Treasury' with no range, no per-share figure, no dividend/buyback split. A board cannot decide on a sized payout from this document, yet the closing slide title says 'Approving the sizing workplan today lets the board decide on a sized payout later' — the deck is a plan to do work, not the analysis. This is the central gap. **Fix:** Add one page that scopes the sizing with the numbers already in the material: surplus = $20.21B cash less a stated minimum reserve, expressed per share on 443.1M shares (slide 5), shown as a range across two or three reserve assumptions (e.g. 1x, 1.5x, 2x the $6.43B capex run-rate). State the reserve basis explicitly and mark it as an assumption to be confirmed.
- The coverage ratio is stated two different ways and neither is tied to a defined measure. Slide 3 title says net income of $9.23B covers capex of $6.43B 'about 1.4 times over', while slide 2 says 'Operating cash flow of $15.82B less capex of $6.43B leaves $9.39B' and separately 'Net income of $9.23B also covers capex about 1.4 times'. The 1.4x is 9.23/6.43 = 1.435, and the 69.7% on slide 3 is 6.43/9.23 = 69.7% — the same ratio restated. Both are admissible, but the deck presents them as if they were different facts, and no denominator is named on the slide. **Fix:** On slide 3, state the ratio as 'Net income / capital expenditure = 9.23 / 6.43 = 1.4x' and add the reciprocal only if it adds information. Remove the duplicate 69.7% line or replace it with a ratio that measures something different (e.g. capex as a percent of operating cash flow: 6.43 / 15.82 = 40.6%).
- Headline cash figure does not tie: slide 2 says 'Cash of $20.21B is 3.1x annual capex of $6.43B', slide 5 says 'cash falls to $15.21B' after the $5B return (20.21 - 5.00 = 15.21, 3 decimals, implying a cash balance of 20.210), but the $5B is called illustrative and rounded while 15.21 is presented to the cent. 20.21 - 5.00 = 15.21 only if cash is exactly 20.210; the 15.21 figure is a hard-coded plug that does not reconcile across slides. **Fix:** Pick one precision: state cash as $20.21B, the return as $5.00B, post-payout as $15.21B on slides 2, 4 and 5; annotate slide 5's $15.21B as 20.21 - 5.00 and tie it to the model cell.
- No valuation content at all. There is no DCF, no comps, no precedents, no football field and not one valuation date or source. Slide 1's title is 'return more cash or keep funding growth' but the deck never says what that growth is worth, what the return on the $6.43B capex is, or what discount rate applies. A board cannot choose between returning $5B and funding growth without the value of a dollar retained versus a dollar returned. **Fix:** Add a valuation page: a football field with at least three methods (DCF on FCF, trading comps, precedent transactions), state WACC, terminal growth and terminal-value share of enterprise value explicitly; show return on incremental capex for the growth option.
- The 'capex stays flat' claim is unsourced and unsupported. Slide 2 says 'Capex of $6.43B takes 69.66 cents of each dollar of $9.23B net income' and slide 3 repeats '$6.43B capex bill', but neither slide gives a prior-year capex number or a management plan for FY2027+ capex. 'Growth stays funded' is asserted, not tested — there is no capex forecast. **Fix:** Add a capex line to slide 2 or 3 with at least FY2025 actual capex and a forecast range, sourced to the 10-K and management guidance, and state the assumption 'capex flat at $6.43B through FY2028.'
- The page title promises 'Three valuation methods test whether retained cash is worth more than the $5B returned' but no method is presented - two of the three figure blocks ($9.39B, $20.21B, 24.7%) are restated cash facts from slides 2-3, and the source line reads 'Valuation ranges: not yet modelled.' There is no DCF, no per-share value of retaining cash, no comparison of retained vs returned dollar. **Fix:** Either build the three methods - (1) DCF of FCF after capex of $9.39B plus terminal value, (2) value of cash retained per share on 443.1M shares vs the $11.28 distributed, (3) reinvestment return on capex at $6.43B/yr - with ranges and a sensitivity on capex/reinvestment return, or delete slide 4 and cut the claim from the title of slide 4 and the 'unless the three methods show a higher value from retaining it' line in the shaded box.
- The entire recommendation rests on a $12.86B floor built as '2 x capex' where capex is $6.43B (2 x 6.43 = 12.86) - a multiple with no peer, policy or covenant basis - and the footnote concedes 'The 2x capex floor and the $5B and $10B sizes are illustrative choices, not facts.' The bar chart plots 20.21 / 15.21 / 12.86 / 10.21 as if 12.86 were an observed constraint. **Fix:** Replace the $12.86B with a board-approved minimum cash policy or a peer/covenant-based floor, show the source, and rebuild the chart and the 'above the floor' comparisons on that number; if no defensible floor exists, state the decision is a judgment call and quantify the downside instead of presenting 12.86 as a threshold.
- Cash does not tie. Slide 4 table shows Operating cash flow 15.82 less Capex 6.43 = 9.39, and 15.82 - 9.39 = 6.43 capex, so operating cash flow after capex leaves $9.39B of period cash generation. Yet slide 2 states 'Cash of $20.21B is 3.1x annual capex of $6.43B' and the $20.21B is carried as a 30 Aug 2026 year-end balance (slide 4 footnote 1). No bridge shows how a single year's $9.39B of free cash flow reconciles to the $20.21B balance, and nothing on any slide reconciles the two. A committee member will ask where the other $10.82B came from and there is no answer in the deck. **Fix:** Add a one-line cash bridge on slide 4 under 'Annual flows': opening cash at 30 Aug 2025 + operating cash flow 15.82 - capex 6.43 +/- other movements = closing cash 20.21, with the opening balance and each movement sourced to the Costco 10-K cash flow statement. If the 10-K does not provide the opening balance, state 'opening balance and other movements not yet extracted from the 10-K' and mark as provisional.
- The slide that is supposed to test 'whether a retained dollar earns more than a returned one' shows DCF as the focus method with status 'Not run', trading comps 'Not run', precedent transactions 'Not run', and three N/A boxes for DCF inputs. Slide 1's recommendation, slide 2's decision box and slide 4's 'So what' all assert the $5B return before any valuation has been performed. Slide 3 footnote 1 says 'Valuation date and source: not available in the sources.' A recommendation cannot precede the analysis it cites as the test. **Fix:** Either (i) run the DCF on free cash flow with the inputs identified (WACC, terminal growth, FY2026-FY2031 forecast cash flows, terminal value as % of total value) plus a trading comps table with named peers, EV/EBITDA and P/E multiples, and a dated precedent transactions table, showing at least three methods; or (ii) if the valuation genuinely cannot be run yet, retitle slide 1 to 'Consider returning up to $5B of $20.21B cash, subject to valuation' and remove the 'Recommend' and 'Approve' language from slides 2 and 5 until the analysis is complete.
- Footnote 1 says 'The facts do not state a free cash flow figure' while the title and body assert '$2.80B of free cash flow'. Both the headline number and its own disclaimer cannot stand. The footnote is the honest one; the headline is the problem. **Fix:** Either delete the $2.80B label and relabel the bar as 'Operating cash flow $15.82B less capex $6.43B' with the arithmetic shown, or state the source line as 'Free cash flow is defined as operating cash flow less capital expenditure; Costco does not report a free cash flow figure — calculated from Costco 10-K filed 2026-10-07 (operating cash flow and capex for the year to 2026-08-30).' Do not title the page on a number the source line disclaims.
- Slide 3 says the return 'leaves about $7.0B of headroom' and 'Ending cash of about $15.0B'. $20.21 + $2.80 - $8.00 = $15.01B, so ending cash is $15.01B, not $15.0B, and headroom over the $8B minimum is $15.01 - $8.00 = $7.01B, not $7.0B. Slide 2 says '$7.0B of cash' left after the return, which is directly inconsistent with slide 3's $15.01B ending cash (slide 2 appears to be headroom, not cash, mislabelled). The same $0.01B appears on slide 4 as 1.9x and slide 3 as 1.9x — the multiple ties but the underlying figures do not. **Fix:** State ending cash as $15.01B on slides 2 and 3, headroom over minimum as $7.01B, and restate slide 2 bullet 1's '$7.0B of cash' as '$7.01B of headroom over the $8B minimum, leaving $15.01B of cash' so the two pages agree to the cent.
- The scoring grid is Harvey balls on a 0-4 scale with no unit on any axis and no valuation method. Footnote 3 says 'Harvey scores are the author's judgement on a 0-4 scale, not company data'. A capital-allocation recommendation to a board cannot rest on author judgement balls; there is no comparison of return on incremental capex, no share-price accretion/dilution, no cost of equity or WACC, and no per-share value of the $8B alternative to retaining it. **Fix:** Replace the Harvey balls with numbers: for each option show ending cash ($B), headroom over the $8B minimum ($B), return per share ($), and the source of funding. Add a section quantifying the $8B as a percentage of market cap and of net income, and the earnings-per-share impact of retiring 5.4M shares, so the board compares dollars to dollars, not balls to balls.
- Slide 3 is titled 'Cash of $20.21B plus $2.80B of free cash flow funds the $8B return', but slide 2 says 'Beginning cash $20.21B plus free cash flow $9.39B ($15.82B operating cash flow less $6.43B capex) funds the return and the plan' and slide 4 footnote 2 says 'Free cash flow of $2.808 is operating cash flow of $15.82B less capex of $6.43B'. FCF cannot be both $2.80B and $9.39B from the same $15.82B minus capex. The $9.39B figure appears to be the uninflated cash before the return; it is mislabeled as free cash flow. **Fix:** Set FCF = operating cash flow $15.82B less capex $6.43B = $9.39B everywhere, and rebuild the slide 3 waterfall on that number; if slide 2 is meant to show pre-return cash, label it 'cash before return', not 'free cash flow'. Before that, tie the operating cash flow and capex figures to the 10-K line items.
- Slide 2 says after the $8B return and the $8B minimum the company 'keeps $13.60B of headroom over the minimum, leaving $21.60B of cash'. Slide 3 and slide 3's callout say ending cash is $15.01B. $20.21B + FCF - $8B cannot equal both $15.01B and $21.60B. The $21.60B figure is unsupported by either the waterfall or the model. **Fix:** Run the bridge once with the correct FCF and state one ending cash figure on slides 2, 3 and 4; delete the $21.60B cash and $13.60B headroom claims or reconcile them explicitly.
- $8B over 443.1M shares is $18.06, the slide 4 figure, which is the whole package: $2B special dividend + $6B buyback. Slide 5 describes the $2B special dividend as 'one-off payment of about $4.51 per share on 443.1M shares'. $2B / 443.1M = $4.51, which is only the dividend leg, yet the slide 4 header calls $18.06 'the $8B package' per share. The buyback leg does not create a per-share payment to all holders. The two pages present mutually inconsistent per-share economics. **Fix:** Either present $18.06 as package value per share across both pages, or show the $2B dividend as $4.51/share and the $6B buyback separately (about 5.4M shares retired at $1,106.21); do not call the buyback a per-share return.
- Arithmetic does not tie to the deck's own bridge. Slide 3 shows 20.21 + 2.80 - 8.00 = 15.01. Slide 2's $7.01B is 15.01 - 8.00 only if you net a minimum twice or otherwise double-count; taken literally the bullet is inconsistent with slide 3's ending cash of $15.01B and with slide 4's 1.9x headroom, which is 15.01/8.00. The same $7.01B appears nowhere else. **Fix:** Replace with the slide-3 figure: 'Ending cash of $15.01B leaves $7.01B of headroom over the $8B minimum' — i.e., state the headroom as exit cash less the $8B minimum, and make the arithmetic 15.01 - 8.00 = 7.01 explicit. Do not show both $15.01B and $7.01B as independent values without the subtraction.
- Double-count of capital returns. Footnote 3 says 'Dividends paid of $1.15B and share repurchases of $848.0M cover the period 1 Sep 2025 to 10 May 2026 (9-month period, not the full FY)' while the $2.80B free cash flow is defined as FY operating cash flow $15.82B less capex $6.43B for the year to 30 Aug 2026. If the $1.15B and $848.0M were already paid out of the FY cash flow, they are inside the $2.80B and cannot also be presented as separate uses of the $20.21B cash without double-counting; if they are being added back, that is not stated. The bridge visually reads 20.21 + 2.80 - 8.00 = 15.01 with no line for them, so the reader cannot reconcile what the $1.15B/$848.0M are doing. **Fix:** Delete the '$1.15B dividends / $848.0M buybacks' bullet or relabel it as 'already reflected in FY cash flow' with a footnote stating whether the figures are excluded from, or additional to, the $2.80B. If the $20.21B cash balance is 30 Aug 2026 and the returns ran 1 Sep 2025-10 May 2026, state explicitly that they are inside the period and therefore inside the $2.80B.
- Implied market cap is $307.7B (8.00/0.026) and implied net income $9.23B (8.00/0.867). Slide 3's own figures (443.1M shares at $1,106.21) give an equity value of roughly $490B and a 0.5% return yield, not 2.6%. One of the two is wrong; the same company cannot have both a $308B and a ~$490B market cap. Net income is sourced to slide 3's K-10 line only through the returns note — the number $9.23B appears nowhere in the deck. **Fix:** Pull the market cap implied by the 443.1M shares at the $1,106.21 close and show the return as a percentage of that; show net income explicitly from the Costco 10-K with the fiscal year, and tie both line items to slide 3's share count and price so the two pages agree. Do not leave two different market-cap/net-income bases on adjacent pages.
- The incremental mandate does not tie to the authorization. Slide 2 item 3 and slide 5 footnote 1 say the 2023 program authorized '$4,000M' with '$1,115M' unspent and the new '$8-10B authorization replaces it, so incremental capacity is $6.9-8.9B'. If the new $8-10B absorbs the $1.115B rollover, incremental authorization is only $6.885-8.885B, so the '$8-10B mandate' headline overstates new capacity — and the executive summary recommends it anyway. **Fix:** State the incremental authorization explicitly on slides 2 and 5 ('new $8-10B authorization; $1,115M remains under the expiring program; incremental capacity $6.9-8.9B'), and either raise the new authorization so incremental capacity is $8-10B or restate the recommendation as a $6.9-8.9B incremental mandate.
- Slide 1 subtitle reads 'Recommend recommend approving a buyback of $8B to $10B, funded from FY2026 free cash flow of $9.38B'; slide 3 title reads 'FY2027 free cash flow of $8.32B funds the buyback' and slide 4's page note refers to 'FY2027 residual.' The deck offers $9.38B/FY2026 and $8.32B/FY2027 as the funding source within two pages. **Fix:** Pick one: fund the buyback from FY2026 residual free cash flow of $9.38B (the figure supported by the $15.82B operating cash flow less $6.43B capex arithmetic on slide 2) or from FY2027 free cash flow of $8.32B, and make slides 1, 3, 4 and 5 read the same number and the same year. If both are relevant, show the roll-forward from $9.38B FY2026 residual to $8.32B FY2027 free cash flow.
- The bridge does not foot. Bars are FY2026 OCF 15.82, FY2026 capex -6.43, FY2026 FCF 9.38, FY2027 capex step-up -1.07, FY2027 FCF 8.32. The step-up using 15.82 - 6.43 - 1.07 = 8.32 is labelled as a 'capex step-up' of -1.07, but the deck says FY2027 capex is $7.50B vs FY2026 $6.43B, a step-up of $1.07B, and FY2027 operating cash flow is 12.6% above FY2026, which is $15.82B x 1.126 = $17.81B, not held flat. The bridge implies FY2027 OCF is unchanged at $15.82B, which contradicts the deck's own 12.6% growth claim. **Fix:** Rebuild the bridge honestly: FY2026 OCF 15.82, less FY2026 capex 6.43, equals FY2026 FCF 9.38; then add FY2027 OCF growth and subtract the $7.50B FY2027 capex to get $8.32B, or state explicitly that FY2027 free cash flow is shown at flat operating cash flow. Show the FY2027 OCF bar so the bridge foots.
- The right-hand box says 'The buyback draws on FY2027 free cash flow of $8.32B, not the FY2026 residual of $9.38B,' while slide 2 the executive summary says 'Capacity: $9.38B of free cash flow after the capital plan' and slide 5 says the mandate 'fits within the $9.38B of free cash flow left after the $6.43B capex plan.' The board will ask which year the $8-10B comes out of and get two answers. **Fix:** Set the funding year and state it once: 'funded from FY2026 residual free cash flow of $9.38B' or 'funded from FY2027 free cash flow of $8.32B,' and carry that same sentence on slides 1, 2, 3, 4 and 5.
- Slide 2 says 'Cash of $20.21B against total debt of $6.16B means no new borrowing.' That is a gross-cash/gross-debt comparison that ignores committed obligations and working-capital seasonality; it reads as a plug to justify 'no new borrowing' instead of a real net-debt and liquidity test. It also conflicts with a board-size authorization ($8-10B) against a cash balance that is not unrestricted. **Fix:** Replace the line with a net-debt test: state net cash/debt as total debt of $6.16B less cash of $20.21B = net cash of $14.05B, then state the available liquidity after the $6.43B FY2026 capex plan and the $8-10B buyback. If a revolver is used, show it explicitly with its drawn/undrawn capacity.
- The coverage ratios as written are wrong. Slide 5 says 'Operating cash flow of $15.82B covers the $6.43B FY2026 plan 2.5x and the $7.50B FY2027 step-up 2.1x.' 15.82/6.43 = 2.46x, not 2.5x (rounding is defensible), but 15.82/7.50 = 2.11x, not 2.1x, and, more importantly, the $15.82B is FY2026 operating cash flow being used as if it covers the FY2027 capex step-up, which is a period mismatch. The deck's own bridge on slide 3 shows FY2026 FCF of $9.38B, so the FY2027 capex is covered by FY2026 FCF of $9.38B at 1.3x, not by the gross operating cash flow. **Fix:** Restate as 'FY2026 operating cash flow of $15.82B covers the $6.43B FY2026 capex plan 2.5x; FY2026 free cash flow of $9.38B covers the ~$7.50B FY2027 capex intention 1.3x.' If you want to show the gross 2.1x, label it as FY2026 OCF to FY2027 capex and flag the period mismatch in the footnote.
- Share retirements don't tie. Slide 2 says the $8B package 'retires 5.42M shares, about 1.22% of the count' (a $6B buyback at $1,106.21 retires 5.42M, and if that is 1.22% the count is ~444M). Slide 4 says the same package retires '7.2M' shares and the buyback alone retires '5.4M shares' equal to '1.6% of shares outstanding'. The $2B special dividend retires no shares at all, so 7.2M counts the dividend as share retirement, and 5.8M/444M is 1.3%, not 1.6%. **Fix:** State one share count on slide 2 and carry it to slide 4: 5.42M shares retired by the $6B buyback (1.22% of 443M shares outstanding per the 10-K cover). Delete '7.2M' from slide 4's table and '1.6% of shares outstanding' from the four-numbers block, or relabel them 'shares retired by buyback'.
- The announced returns are never reconciled in dollars. Slide 5 says 'Set the announced $30 per share special dividend and $4B buyback against the proposed total so the board sees one number, not two' and states at the bottom '$30 per share special dividend and the $4B buyback authorization are announced items, not proposals', but no page computes the $30 x 443M = ~$13.3B, nor the $4B buyback, nor the $17.3B announced total versus the proposed $8B. Slide 2 says the package 'is the incremental cash-on-hand case and must be reconciled with those announced actions' but never reconciles it. **Fix:** Add a reconciliation block to slide 5: announced $30/share special dividend x 443M shares outstanding (per the 10-K cover) = ~$13.3B, plus announced $4B buyback = ~$17.3B; proposed $8B package is incremental to / replaces part of that, showing the residual. Use only the 443M count already in the deck.
- The $6B buyback is credited with retiring 5.42M shares and the $2B with paying $4.51/share, and the EPS effect is quoted as 'about 1.2%' in both places. Check the arithmetic: 5.42M shares on 443.1M is 1.22% of the count, but the EPS effect of retiring 1.22% of shares is 1.22%/(1-0.0122) = 1.24% only if net income is unchanged — and net income does not fall, because the slide also says the buyback is funded from cash earning 1.88%. Confirm that the 1.2% is a diluted EPS percentage and not the share-retirement percentage quoted as EPS; if it is the latter, state it as such. **Fix:** Add one line under slide 2 row 4 stating the EPS bridge in $: pre-buyback EPS $20.76 (net income / 443.1M shares), post-buyback EPS at the same net income divided by 437.68M shares, and show the difference in dollars per share as well as the percent. Tie both the percent and the dollar amount to the same share count.
- The option grid is unreadable and does not sum. Column headings are truncated ('Operat ing cash flow cover', 'Capex cover after' / 'return'), the second and third option rows are cut off by the 'The $8B return in four numbers' banner, and the visible row shows 'Held all $20.21B' with '3.1x' capex cover and '2.5x' operating cash flow cover — the same numbers the deck elsewhere attributes to the return case. A committee cannot tell which option produces which score. **Fix:** Rebuild the table with full column labels (Option; Capex cover after return; Operating cash flow cover; Cash retained; Shares retired; EPS effect; Score), one row per option (Hold all; $8B return; announced $30 dividend + $4B buyback), and make the numbers in each row foot to slide 3's cash bridge. Move the four-number banner below the table so it stops covering the rows.
- Share retirement is internally inconsistent. Slide 2 says 'The $6B buyback retires 5.42M shares' and 'The $2B special dividend pays about $4.51 a share'; slide 4 repeats 'The $6B buyback retires 5.42M shares at $1,106.21'. At $1,106.21, $6B / $1,106.21 = 5.42M shares — the arithmetic in the sentence counts the $6B only, so the $8B return retires the same 5.42M, meaning the $2B dividend adds no shares. Either the share count is the $6B figure (and the $2B is cash, not shares), or the 5.42M is misstated for the $8B package. As written the 1.22% retirement and the EPS bridge on slide 4 cannot be reproduced. **Fix:** State the buyback share count as the $6B / $1,106.21 = 5.42M figure and delete the implication it is the $8B; or if the 5.42M is intended as the $8B package, restate as '$8B at $1,106.21 retires 7.23M shares' and rerun the EPS bridge. Footnote the price and date.
- The EPS accretion is asserted, not calculated. Slide 2 says 'Retiring 5.42M shares lifts EPS from $20.76 to about $21.01, a gain of about $0.25 a share' and slide 4 says 'lifts EPS about 1.2%'. $0.25 / $20.76 = 1.20% only if the buyback is funded by retiring shares with no lost interest income. Slide 3 itself says 'the $8B stops earning interest', and slide 2 notes the buyback earns 'a 1.88% earnings yield vs 5.15% Treasury'. The foregone interest is charged nowhere in the $21.01. The 'before lost interest income' qualifier on slide 2 admits this but slide 4's headline does not. **Fix:** Rebuild the EPS bridge with both lines: pro forma EPS = (reported net income - after-tax interest foregone on $6B + incremental dividend cost) / (443.1M - 5.42M). Show the $0.25 gross and the net figure after interest foregone; state the resulting % as the headline on slide 4 or relabel it 'before lost interest income'.
- The $8.0B return does not tie to the recomputable components. $8.0B less the $4.0B buyback leaves $4.0B for the special dividend, but $4.0B / 443.1M = $9.03, not the $9.03 stated ($9.03 x 443.1M = $4.002B). The distribution is stated to four significant figures but does not foot to $8.0B. Either $8.00B is rounded or the components are wrong; a board item cannot have both. **Fix:** Fix the arithmetic basis and relabel. If the special is $4.00B, state the per-share dividend as $9.03 and note it sums to $8.00B, not $8.0B. Show a tie-out table: $4.00B buyback + $4.00B special = $8.00B, or restate the return as $8.03B.
- The 327bp shortfall is presented as the case against the buyback, but it is an FX translation, not an economic cost. The buyback is in USD; the company earns 22.2% after tax on its own reinvested capital (slide 4, right column, "The business earns 22.2% on capital"). Setting a dollar buyback against a dollar Treasury yield proves only that execution is USD-denominated; the relevant test is the return on the incremental project versus the return on the shares, both in the company's functional currency, not the bond denomination. **Fix:** Replace the Treasury comparison (and the $131M holding-cost line on slide 4) with an ordinary incremental-capital comparison: marginal return on the $6.43B new capex versus the after-tax cost of equity, or the spread of ROIC over WACC. Use the after-tax cost of debt already available in the model. Keep the $4.00B buyback cap only if the incremental project's risk-adjusted return exceeds the buyback's, and show the math.
- The cap-the-buyback argument compares a 1.88% earnings yield to a 5.15% Treasury yield and calls the 327bp spread 'the cost of the decision'/'forgoes about $131M a year.' That is not a valuation: it values the equity on a no-growth earnings-yield basis while ignoring buyback accretion to EPS, the after-tax cost of the alternative, and the fact that the $131M is a pre-tax spread on a nominal-yield basis against a risk-free alternative with different risk. Slide 4's own footnote 1 concedes 'the 22.2% is historical and cannot rank the new capex against the buyback,' so the exhibit's headline ranking is unsupported by its own sources. **Fix:** Recast slide 4 as a capital-allocation comparison: (a) after-tax earnings yield vs after-tax cost of capital (or the marginal return on the $6.43B new capex, which slide 4 admits is not in the sources), and (b) EPS accretion of the $4.00B tranche retiring 3.6M shares. Delete the '$131M a year' figure unless it is re-derived on an after-tax basis and labelled as such.
- Two different close dates/values are used for the same input in one deck and one of them is mislabelled: slide 4 source says 'Yahoo Finance COST close 2026-11-13' with $1,106.21, slide 2 source says 'Yahoo Finance ... TNX, 2026-11-13,' and slide 5 source says 'TNX, 2026-11-11.' The deck is dated November 2026 board meeting; using a Nov 2026 close in a November 2026 board deck is contemporaneous only if the date is right. As rendered, the TNX/dates differ by page and cannot be reconciled to a single market snapshot. **Fix:** Fix one market snapshot for the whole deck (state the exact date and time) and use it on slides 2, 4 and 5; correct the slide 4 source line and the slide 5 source line to the same date.

### Deck: Senior partner (86)

- The 'ask' is 'Confirm the growth capex plan / Confirm debt, leases and maturities / Propose a floor method, not a level / Choose the payout form' — all owned by the CFO, all 'before next board meeting'. This is a homework list, not a decision. The board cannot approve 'set a floor' when the floor level is unknown; the deck's own headline (slides 1 and 2) promises a number. **Fix:** Rewrite slide 5's ask to name the two things the board actually decides today: (1) 'Approve the principle: capex funded first, return only cash above the floor' and (2) 'Approve the sizing rule: floor = near-term maturities + minimum operating cash + seasonal swing'. Keep the CFO actions as a separate 'what we will bring back' line.
- The title claims '$2.80B of earnings' but the chart labels the third bar 'Earnings left after capex' and the footnote admits 'The $2.80B is a comparison, not free cash flow. Total debt, lease obligations and maturities are in the material and are unverified.' The page presents a hard number the deck itself flags as unverified. **Fix:** Change the third bar label to 'Net income less capex (unverified, excludes debt and leases)' and add a one-line note under the title: 'This is not free cash flow; $20.21B gross cash is offset by unconfirmed debt, leases and maturities.' Or cut the $2.80B bar until treasury confirms it.
- Capex of $6.43B is 69.66% of $9.23B net income, so earnings left after capex are $2.80B. But the subtitle says 'leaving $2.80B of earnings' and the sidebar says '$2.80B of earnings remains after capex'. That is a subtraction, not earnings remaining; it is the difference between net income and capex and is not a cash return pool. Naming it 'earnings' makes the board think $2.80B is available to return. **Fix:** Retitle slide 3 to 'Fiscal 2026 capex took 69.66% of net income, leaving $2.80B of net income after capex' and relabel the $2.80B figure as 'residual income after capex' throughout. Delete the word 'earnings' on slide 3.
- The page claims cash is unconfirmed and 'surplus cannot be sized yet', but the chart presents cash, net income and capex as three comparable bars. The visual contradicts the caveat and lets a reader treat cash as free. **Fix:** Replace the three-bar chart with a waterfall: net income $9.23B minus capex $6.43B = $2.80B residual, then show cash $20.21B as a separate, labelled balance with the note 'unconfirmed until treasury reports debt, leases and maturities'. Remove the 3.14x coverage framing until that confirmation exists.
- Title says growth earns '7.2x the 3.10% idle cash earns' but slide 2 says '5.4x the Treasury yield'. The supporting math on slide 2 footnotes gives 22.2%/4.12% = 5.4x pre-tax, while 22.2%/3.10% = 7.2x after-tax. Both appear, unlabelled as pre- vs after-tax, so the headline multiple changes between pages. **Fix:** Pick one basis for the multiple and use it everywhere. Slide 2 bullet 1 should read 'earns 7.2x the 3.10% after-tax return on idle cash' to match slide 3, and the '5.4x the Treasury yield' phrase should be deleted or relabelled '5.4x the 4.12% pre-tax T-bill yield'.
- The chart shows Residual at -0.59 and the page title says 'Cash of $20.21B covers the $13.3B dividend and $7.5B capex plan' while the bullet below states 'Residual cash after both uses is -$0.59B, so the two uses exceed the cash balance'. Title and evidence contradict. The negative residual bar is also cut off at the chart edge and its label is unreadable. **Fix:** Change slide 3's title to 'Cash of $20.21B plus $9.38B FY26 free cash flow funds the $13.3B dividend and $7.5B capex; stock cash falls $0.59B', relabel the bar '-0.59' inside the plot, and set the bar axis so the negative bar is fully visible.
- The headline 'Growth returns 22.2% after tax, 11.8x the 1.88% a buyback earns' compares a whole-company after-tax return on book capital (footnote 1: 'equity $35.80B + long-term debt $3.91B') against a 1.88% earnings yield, and the page itself admits the 1.88% 'excludes interest lost on the cash spent' and is 'an earnings yield (EPS / price), not a return on invested capital'. That is not the same basis, so the 11.8x multiple is not a like-for-like comparison. **Fix:** Recompute both sides on the same basis and state it in the headline. Either put both as returns on capital employed (the 22.2% basis), or both as yields to the shareholder; then rewrite the slide 4 headline to the single number that survives, e.g. 'Growth earns 22.2% on capital; a buyback earns [X]% on the same basis' and move the method into footnote 1.
- The recommendation is 'Approve the $4B buyback authorisation today' but slide 3 says 'The $4B is the authorisation, not a return-maximising choice' and slide 4 says 'surplus cash belongs in warehouses' with a 1.88% buyback yield against 22.2% growth. The deck argues against its own ask. **Fix:** Decide the recommendation. If the ask is renewal of the authorisation as a housekeeping resolution, write the titles that way: slide 1 'Renew the $4B repurchase authorisation, which lapses January 2027; the $30 special dividend is already declared'. If the ask is deployment, drop the buyback ask and title the deck to the growth case. Do not carry both.
- The title claims urgency ('must renew it today') but the deck never states what breaks if the board waits. The notes say the prior authorisation approved January 2023 expires January 2027, six weeks after this meeting - no consequence is given. **Fix:** Either give the consequence in one line on slide 3 ('without renewal the programme lapses and the remaining authorisation cannot be executed in Q1 2027') or change the title to the actual decision, 'The board is asked to renew the $4B repurchase authorisation that lapses January 2027'.
- The two numbers are not on the same basis and the page says so in its own footnotes: 22.2% is a company-level after-tax return on book capital (equity $35.8B + long-term debt $3.9B), while 1.88% is an earnings yield (EPS/price) and the footnote states it 'excludes interest lost on the cash spent'. Comparing a debt-inclusive return on equity and debt to an unlevered earnings yield overstates the growth advantage. **Fix:** Put both on one basis. State the basis in the title ('Warehouse capital earns 22.2% after tax; cash held for buyback earns 1.88% after tax, both unlevered') or recompute growth returns on the same capital base. If a like-for-like figure cannot be produced, remove the comparison and present the buyback yield alone against the cost of cash.
- The two bars, 'Warehouse growth (after-tax return on capital, FY2026)' at 22.20% and 'Cash (US 10-year Treasury yield, 2026-11-13)' at 5.15%, are not like-for-like; footnote 1 admits this ('The two bars are not like-for-like'), which undercuts the explicit title 'Growth earns 22.2% on capital, cash earns 5.15%'. **Fix:** Either (a) retitle to 'Growth returned 22.2% after tax vs 5.15% on cash in FY2026, but the forward warehouse return is not disclosed' and add the words 'FY2026 actual, not forward' on the growth bar, or (b) drop the bar for cash and present the 17.1-point gap as a single figure with the 5.15% rate as a reference line. Do not leave a footnote that contradicts the title.
- The 22.2% return on capital is a claim in the title and the case for the dividend, but the underlying figures ($11.69B operating income, $35.80B capital base) appear only in a footnote. The page needs a bridge table or a two-column reconciliation showing the calculation. **Fix:** Add a small proof table under the bars: after-tax operating income $11.69B / capital $35.80B = 22.2%; and $1 x 5.15% = 5.15%. Show the arithmetic on the page.
- The recommendation contradicts itself. Slide 1 says 'Approve the $7.5B expansion'; slide 2 says 'Approve the expansion as the priority use of capital'; slide 3 says 'Require a project-level return case before approving the expansion'. A board cannot approve and withhold approval on the same page sequence. **Fix:** Pick one answer. Recommended: make the ask conditional and consistent — slide 2 becomes 'Conditional approval: authorize $7.5B subject to a warehouse-level return case', slide 3's title becomes the condition it already argues ('No project-level return exists: the 22.2% is existing-book, not marginal'), and slide 5's next step 1 remains the gating item.
- Item 3 reads 'Defer repurchases' with support 'The $4B buyback returns -3.27% versus Treasury.' A negative return versus Treasury is an argument for pausing, not deferring, and the word 'defer' hides the recommendation. The evidence and the action do not match. **Fix:** Change the action line to 'Keep the $4B buyback authorization inactive' and the support to 'Authorization is $4B; trailing return is -3.27% versus Treasury' — this matches slide 5, step 4.
- The 'Evidence or gap' column admits the answer is missing: 'Project return: N/A: not available'. The page therefore does not prove its own title and the single most important number in the decision (the return on the $7.5B) is absent from the deck. **Fix:** Either obtain the warehouse-level return (see research request) and put it in the cell, or retitle the page 'The $7.5B has no disclosed return: the 22.2% is existing-book, not marginal' and make the missing number the explicit reason for the condition. Do not leave a cell that says the evidence does not exist under a title that asserts a conclusion.
- Slide 1 asserts 'FY2026 free cash flow covered the FY2027 expansion plan' and the subtext states 'FY2026 free cash flow was $9.38B versus $7.50B of planned expansion spend.' Slide 4's title says 'The FY2026 free cash flow surplus over the FY2027 capex plan is unverified' and its table shows 'Cash from operations N/A; FY2026' with the note that free cash flow cannot be tested. The deck opens on a claim it later retracts. **Fix:** Delete the $9.38B figure and the coverage claim from slide 1. Make slide 1's title the verified position, e.g. 'FY2026 free cash flow cannot be verified from disclosed sources' or, if you have the cash flow statement, the actual number. Align slides 1, 2 and 4 to one claim.
- Point 2 'Cash generation covers planned uses' with '$24.8B; cash plus free cash flow cover them, leaving $4.79B' asserts exactly the coverage that slide 4 says is unverified. The executive summary asks the board to approve on evidence the deck then discredits. **Fix:** Replace point 2's claim with the verified arithmetic only (planned uses $24.8B against cash on hand and the unverified operating cash flow), or move to 'Test the coverage before release.' Do not resolve $24.8B as covered on page 2 while page 4 says it cannot be tested.
- Slide 2's decision box says 'approve the FY2027 warehouse budget and defer the buyback,' but slide 5's next step 1 is 'Provide IRR or payback for all 33 sites' gated 'Before FY2027 capital is released.' The deck asks for approval and simultaneously asks to withhold it. **Fix:** Choose the actual ask. If the recommendation is to approve subject to a gate, say so on page 2: 'Approve the $7.5B in principle, release tranches only on site-level IRR evidence.' Then slide 5's gate is a condition of the ask, not a contradiction.
- The grid is labelled 'Option A', 'Option B', 'option C' in the takeaway box, but the page never defines A, B or C. The body shows cash-cover lines and a headline recommendation, not options. A director reading page 4 cold cannot tell what A and B are, which makes the page's title ('Option A is affordable...') unreadable. **Fix:** Add an explicit option legend at the top of slide 4: 'Option A = ratify the $17.29B return; Option B = ratify and re-scope capex; Option C = defer the return.' Then make each row of the table map to one option, and re-title the page 'Ratifying the $17.29B return is affordable from cash at 1.17x; re-scoping capex breaches the 3.88% hurdle.'
- The equity-invested-capital baseline is internally inconsistent: slide 3's callout says $39.71B invested capital while the table beside it says equity $35.80B + long-term debt $3.91B = $39.71B, and slide 1/slide 2 show share count 443.1M at $1,106.21, implying a market cap of about $490.2B — yet the 22.2% return and 4.12% cash cost produce a buyback return of 1.88% on $20.76 EPS that assumes buybacks are executed at the market price, not the invested-capital base. The page must state clearly whether the 1.88% is cash-on-cash at the market price or against the equity book, because the two produce different answers. **Fix:** On slide 3, add a single sentence under the 1.88% box: '1.88% = $20.76 EPS / $1,106.21 market price, i.e., the return on repurchases executed at today's market price, not on the equity book.' Then relabel the right-hand table row 'Existing book' as 'Equity book (ex-leases)' and show the reconciliation to the 22.2% so the reader can trace the two numbers.
- The cell reads 'Not yet sourced' and 'No marginal after-tax return per new unit is in the facts; open research item.' That is an open research gap printed on the board page that carries the central comparison; a director cannot approve a plan whose return is literally blank. **Fix:** Either (a) source the marginal after-tax return per new warehouse from the FY2026 10-K segment disclosure and put the number in, or (b) if the number is genuinely unavailable, remove the 22.2% vs 1.88% headline and reframe the page as a capital-cost comparison only, and move the plan-return item to slide 5 as the first research action with an owner and date.
- The right column overlaps itself. 'Cash generation and the cost of holding it' sits on top of two numbers; the $6.03B and $1.27B figures (cash flow year to 30 Aug 2026, and the next block) are half-obscured, and $3.45B reads only as '6.03B'/'3.45B' fragments. A committee cannot read a page that is covering its own numbers. **Fix:** Rebuild slide 3's right column: reflow into two clean rows of two tiles each, drop the two tiles that duplicate the left column, and re-render to confirm no number is clipped.
- The $6.43B tile is labelled 'FY2026 capex: actual for the year to 30 Aug 2026' while slide 2 and the left chart say $4,228M spent at week 36 / $4.23B to 10 May 2026. Two different 'actual capex for the year' on consecutive pages. One of them is wrong. **Fix:** Pick one figure. If $6.43B is the full-year FY2026 capex estimate and $4.23B is the 10 May cash-spend to date, label them 'FY2026 capex, full year (estimated)' and 'spent to 10 May 2026' respectively, and put both on the same basis on slides 2 and 4.
- The page gives two different cash hurdle rates: the title and body say the buyback at 1.88% is 'below the 4.12% 13-week T-bill yield', but the callout says 'the buyback fails the 4.12% cash rate test' while slide 2 footnote 1 defines the after-tax hurdle as 3.10% (4.12% x (1 - 24.7%)). The comparison is pre-tax yield against post-tax hurdle. A committee member will catch this in ten seconds and it undermines the whole page. **Fix:** Pick one basis and state it once. Use the after-tax 3.10% hurdle from slide 2: 'Buyback earnings yield 1.88% vs 3.10% after-tax hurdle' and delete the 4.12% references from the body and callout. If you keep 4.12%, say 'pre-tax' explicitly and show 1.88% grossed up on the same basis.
- The $6,500M / $4,228M / 29 / 22.2% callout boxes sit flush against the title bar with no top gap, the left grid starts higher than the right 'What the plan buys' box, and the two cells are visibly unequal widths — the page reads as an untidy Excel paste, not a board exhibit. The right box is a wall of four bullets with no visual. **Fix:** Align the left edge of the grid with the title and the right box; equalise the four callout cells to a 2×2 of identical width and height; add a 24px top margin under the title bar. On the right, replace the four-bullet wall with a single small bar or line chart — FY23A–FY26A capex $4.32B to $6.43B against operating cash flow $15.82B — and keep at most two bullets. The dark footnote bar on slide 4 is the model; make slide 3's footnotes look like slide 4's.
- The exhibit is titled 'Earnings yield at three prices' but plots only three data points as three giant numbers down the left half, with the right half half-empty and the real content crammed into a dark box in the bottom corner. A reader cannot see the relationship between price and yield; that is the entire argument. **Fix:** Replace the three stacked numbers with a single line or column chart: price on the x-axis from $600 to $1,150, earnings yield on the y-axis, a horizontal line at the 3.10% hurdle, and mark $850.00 (2.44%), $1,106.21 (1.88%) and the $669.68 crossing. Keep the takeaway 'Buyback clears the hurdle only at or below $669.68' as the chart subtitle. Move the call-out box content into the page body; drop the box.
- The EPS figure conflicts. Slide 2 says 'each $1B adds 4 cents of EPS.' Slide 4 says 'each $1B of buyback reduces EPS by 3 cents' and footnotes the net effect as '-$0.03 per $1B' at '4.12%' pre-tax interest. Same decision, two opposite answers. **Fix:** Reconcile the two. The bridge on slide 4 is the arithmetic that can be checked (4 cents EPS less 7 cents interest forgone at 4.12% = net -3 cents), so correct slide 2 to match, or if slide 2 is using a different tax/basis, state the basis in the title. The two pages must give the board one number.
- The bridge does not tie. Bars read +15.82 operating, -6.43 capex, -1.15 dividends, -13.30 special, -1.00 buyback, showing 14.15 closing against 20.21 opening. That sums to 20.21+15.82-6.43-1.15-13.30-1.00 = 14.15. It balances, but the title says $15.45B returns and $9.38B FCF draw $6.07B — those numbers are not on the page and don't reconcile with the 14.15 closing. The reader cannot verify the claim from the chart. **Fix:** Add the returns and FCF figures as labelled callouts beside the relevant bars so the title ties to the picture, or restate the title to the number the chart actually proves ('$6.07B of cash retires: 20.21 opening falls to 14.15 closing after the $13.3B special dividend').
- The buyback case is run at the 2026-11-13 close of $1,106.21, yet the same footnote concedes a cost of equity range of 6.11% to 6.59%, and the box says only that 'at $900 each $1B adds $0.05 of EPS'. The break-even share price is never stated, so the board cannot see how much headroom the $0.03 conclusion has. **Fix:** Compute and print the break-even price at which $1B of buyback adds zero value, and put it in the slide 4 right-hand box next to the $900 case: 'value-neutral above $X a share; at the 2026-11-13 close of $1,106.21 each $1B destroys $0.03'. Do not introduce a new price; solve for the one implied by the 4.12% T-bill and 24.7% tax rate already on the page.
- Slide 2 says the $13.3B special dividend is 'declared and fully funded' and shows a single $13.3B box, but slide 1 says '$30 special dividend' and slide 4's right panel says '$20.21B plus free cash flow $9.38B covers capex $6.5B and ordinary dividends' — the $13.3B special dividend is not in that coverage list, and the two pages disagree on what is being funded. **Fix:** Pick one funding statement and use it everywhere: on slide 4, replace the right-panel bullet with 'Cash $20.21B + FCF $9.38B covers the $13.3B special dividend, $6.5B capex and ordinary dividends' — or if it does not, delete 'fully funded' on slide 2 and state the gap.
- The $670 floor is asserted, never derived. Slide 2 says 'Below $670 the earnings yield exceeds 3.10%' and slide 5 repeats '$670 per share' with no arithmetic on the page; the only derivation is footnote 2 in 6-point type. **Fix:** Put the one-line derivation on slide 2 next to the $670 box: '$670 = $20.76 EPS ÷ 3.10% after-tax cash yield'. Move it out of footnote 2.
- The title ends in an ellipsis and the page never resolves it — 'Capex has grown 14.2% a year against 7.8% revenue growth…' — while the right column says the plan 'holds' only 'while after-tax return on capital stays 22.2%'. That is a conditional, not an answer, and slide 3's footnote 3 admits the marginal return 'is not disclosed in the filings reviewed nor available in the sources'. **Fix:** Retitle to the actual claim: 'Capex grew 14.2% a year vs 7.8% revenue; the $6.5B plan holds only if return on capital stays 22.2%'. Put that conditional in a callout box so the reader cannot miss it.
- The right half is prose and a callout, not evidence: 'Capex is compounding at 14.2% a year' and 'The plan is funded and largely in the ground' are restated in text while the bar chart only covers the left half. The page's title claim ('holds only while return on capital stays 22.2%') is never proved with data — the 22.2% tie to capex is the whole argument and it is asserted in a bullet. **Fix:** Split the page: keep the three-bar CAGR chart as one exhibit, and add a second visual that plots capex CAGR against return on capital over FY23–FY26 (with a 14.2% reference line) so the reader can see the 22.2% hold. Replace the sentence bullets with that chart plus one 8-word takeaway.
- The tax-rate derivation is internally inconsistent. Slide 2 footnote 1 defines the effective tax rate as tax expense $3.028B / net income $9.238B = 24.7%, but the Washington Post source label on slide 2 says '24.7% effective tax rate' and slide 4 shows '24.7% Effective tax rate'. A $13.3B dividend plus $6.5B capex is $19.8B against $20.21B cash plus $9.38B free cash flow of $29.59B — that part reconciles, but the 1.88% vs 3.10% gap that drives the whole recommendation rests entirely on the 24.7% rate. If that rate is wrong, the $670 floor is wrong. **Fix:** Check the 24.7% against the 10-K income tax note and show the calculation on the slide: state pre-tax T-bill 4.12%, tax at 24.7%, after-tax 3.10%. Add the pre-tax-to-after-tax bridge as a visible line so a reader can verify $670 = $20.76 / 3.10% without a footnote.
- The page is empty. Only the title 'Historical returns support screening growth projects before buybacks' renders; the grid that proves it is missing. A blank page in a five-page deck is a hole in the argument. **Fix:** Build the grid: rows = years or capital-allocation cycles, columns = growth capex / buyback / dividend, cells = returns. If the underlying data does not exist in the material, cut slide 3 and fold its point into slide 4 rather than shipping a blank.
- Slide 2 says 'Prioritize growth projects' while slides 1, 4 and 5 say 'approve $0 of additional discretionary returns.' The deck answers two different questions. 'Prioritize growth' is not the same decision as 'authorize $0 of returns.' **Fix:** Make one question the spine: does the board approve $0 of incremental buybacks/dividends through FY2027, with growth capex funded from the $20.21B cash and $15.82B operating cash flow? Rewrite slide 2's title to 'Approve $0 in additional returns through FY2027; fund growth from the $20.21B cash balance' and make slides 3-4 prove that.
- The big number $15.82B is labelled 'FY2026 operating cash flow,' but slide 4 says 'Operating cash flow reached $15.82B.' Slide 2 also stacks '2.31%%' — a double percent sign that is a typo in the hero number. **Fix:** Fix '2.31%%' to '2.31%' on slide 2. Confirm $15.82B is FY2026 operating cash flow on slide 4 and use the same label on both pages.
- 'Fund growth projects only after their forecast return clears a sourced hurdle' sits at the top of an otherwise blank page. No grid, no table, no figures, no chart — roughly 90% dead space. **Fix:** Build the grid you promised. Rows = candidate projects with the return each must clear; columns = forecast return, sourced hurdle, source of the hurdle, pass/fail. Populate every cell from the underlying analysis, or drop the page.
- 'Keep additional returns at $0 through FY2027 until funding needs are clear' is a title on an empty page. Nothing proves the claim. **Fix:** Show the funding-needs bridge: sources and uses FY2027 with the $20.21B cash and $15.82B FY2026 operating cash flow as the starting point, and mark the $0 returns line in it. If the numbers don't exist, this page cannot ship.
- 'Approve $0 in additional discretionary returns while we verify the evidence' is a title with no ask, no owners, no dates. **Fix:** Make it a real closing page: restate the decision in one line, then a table of next steps — action, owner, date — for the liquidity verification, the project hurdle sourcing, and the funding-needs test. Use only dates and names you actually have.
- The same message four times: dive, exec summary, slide 4 title and slide 5 all say approve $0 through FY2027. After page 1 the reader has the answer and gets nothing new. **Fix:** Keep page 1 and page 2. Rewrite 3, 4 and 5 so each advances the argument: 3 = the hurdle test, 4 = the funding-needs evidence, 5 = the ask with owners and dates. Delete the repeated sentence.
- Page is titled 'Test project returns against the required hurdle before committing cash' and contains nothing else — an empty page. No hurdle rate, no project list, no returns, no comparison. **Fix:** Build slide 3 as a project-by-project table: each named warehouse/capex project, IRR or ROI, the hurdle rate as a column, pass/fail, and capital requested; add one line at the foot giving the total capital that clears the hurdle and the total that does not.
- Page is titled 'Hold additional returns until liquidity and project tests are met' and contains nothing else — an empty page. The liquidity tests are never defined anywhere in the deck. **Fix:** Put the test definition on the page: minimum cash floor, coverage ratio or leverage trigger, and the specific project hurdle; show current value versus required value for each so the reader sees whether the gate is already passed or not.
- Closing page is a single sentence with no ask, no owners, no dates. 'Complete obligation and investment checks before the board decides on cash returns' is a task description, not a decision. **Fix:** Replace with an action table: three to five rows, what is to be done (funding test, liquidity test, obligation confirmation), owner by role, due date, and the board decision date they feed. Restate the ask in one line above the table.
- The recommendation is '$0 in additional returns through FY2027' but the page never says what the cash would otherwise buy: no warehouse count, no capex total, no growth plan that competes with the returns for the same dollars. **Fix:** Add one row or a right-hand figure block showing planned FY2027 warehouse openings and the capex tied to them, so the trade-off between returns and growth is visible on the exec summary. Use only figures already in the material or flag as a research request.
- 9.41 is internally inconsistent with the deck's own numbers. Slide 2 and slide 5 both carry $6.5B free cash flow against a $13.3B special dividend, which is a ~$6.8B shortfall; the table implies cash only falls from $20.21B to $9.41B, i.e. a $10.8B fall. The row mixes the starting cash snapshot with the annual dividend and the $4B buyback without a single, stated basis. **Fix:** Rebuild the table on one basis. State the basis in the header: 'starting cash $20.21B, less $13.3B special dividend, less $4B illustrative buyback'. If the intended basis is the post-dividend cash snapshot, the header must say so and the consensus input row must reconcile to the $6.5B free cash flow figure used on slide 2.
- Three of four rows read 'N/A not available' — cash before dividend, other inflows and outflows, and the orange-highlighted 'Cash for warehouse funding' row. The argument of the deck is that payment-date cash is unknown, but a table where the answer is 'N/A' three times proves nothing and the reader has no anchor. Worse, the 'Cash for warehouse funding' line is the one you most need, and it is blank with an alarm colour on it. **Fix:** Rebuild slide 3 around the one number that is real: '$13.3B special dividend, December 4, 2026, versus $20.21B reported August cash.' Make the takeaway explicit — reported cash covers the dividend but the payment-date warehouse funding requirement is unquantified — and put the forecast gap as a single callout, not three N/A rows. If the forecast truly cannot be built, say so in one line and make that the point, not a table of blanks.
- Four of five pages carry the same claim in different words: 'Defer use of the $4B buyback…' (1), 'The board should defer…' (2), 'Defer the $4B buyback…' (4), '…before the board revisits the authorization' (5). There is no progression, so the storyline is a loop, not an argument. **Fix:** Keep slide 2 as the answer page and make 1, 3, 4 each prove a distinct supporting claim with a title that is not the recommendation: e.g. 1 = 'Cash falls from $13.3B to $2.91B if the full buyback is executed', 4 = 'Funding availability, authorization terms and purchase conditions are all unverified'. Slide 5 stays as the ask.
- The central evidence is not in the material. Slide 1 footnote 3 says 'Authorizing resolution: N/A not available in the sources.' Slide 4's Defer row says 'Terms unverified: Reported expiry: November 2030; resolution and capacity: N/A not available.' You are asking the board to defer on the strength of an authorization whose terms you have not read. **Fix:** Get the resolution and the authorising board action, put the expiry, capacity and remaining headroom on slide 4 in the table, and delete every 'N/A not available in the sources' cell. If it cannot be obtained, the title becomes 'Authorization terms and funding availability cannot be verified from public filings' and the ask is disclosure, not deferral.
- The subhead tells the reader 'The trough, not the opening balance, sets the pace,' but no trough is shown; the chart runs from opening balance to year-end. The deck's own footnote 2 admits this: 'the trough balance is not shown as a point figure.' The page states the conclusion it does not prove. **Fix:** Add the intra-year trough as a dated marker on the waterfall between 'Cash at 30 Aug 2026' and the year-end bar, label the month and the balance, and state the trough figure in the subhead; if the trough figure is not yet computed, this becomes the top research request and the subhead must not claim to present it.
- Two bars (20.21 and 7.50) on a 45% empty page — massive dead space between and around the chart, and the right column is a dense bullet wall that duplicates the chart's message. The page needs a voice-over to connect the columns. **Fix:** Replace the two-bar chart with a horizontal cash waterfall or stacked bar that shows $20.21B split into the $7.5B capex, the $4.0B return, and the $8.71B residual buffer in one visual. Move the 'minimum operating cash $3.968B' line onto that chart as a threshold marker. Cut the right-column bullets to two lines maximum: the buffer, and that idle cash earns 4.12%.
- The page is two pages in one: the title claims coverage ('Operating cash flow of $15.82B covers the $7.5B FY2027 capex plan') while the right column reverses it ('...but the $4B buyback renewal and $2B dividend are options, not yet funded'). A board reader gets two messages and no so-what. **Fix:** Split into two pages or make one claim: title slide 3 'Operating cash flow of $15.82B funds the $7.5B FY2027 capex 2.1x over' and move the 'options, not yet funded' point to slide 2's cash walk, or to a new page 3b titled 'The $6B return is funded from surplus, not the capex budget'.
- The chart compares FY2023 ($11.07B/$4.32B) with FY2026 ($15.82B/$6.43B) and silently skips FY2024 and FY2025 ('not shown: those figures are not in hand'). That gap is exactly what a board member will ask about, and the 12.6% growth claim rests on it. **Fix:** Add FY2024 and FY2025 operating cash flow and capex bars, or replace the bars with a single statement chart: FY2026 operating cash flow $15.82B vs FY2027 capex $7.5B, with the 2.1x coverage called out. Do not show a two-point time series and call it a trend.
- The title says operating cash flow of $15.82B covers the $7.5B capex, but the chart plots a single FY2026 operating-cash-flow bar against the FY2027 capex bar. That proves a ratio, not coverage. Coverage is a free-cash-flow question: capex is paid after operating and investing activity, and the page itself concedes the surplus measure is $9.38B FY2026 free cash flow. A buy-side analyst will say the chart is mismatched to the claim. **Fix:** Replace the two-bar chart with a single stacked waterfall or bridge: FY2026 operating cash flow $15.82B, less the $9.38B free cash flow line or less maintenance capex, arriving at the $7.5B FY2027 capex with the leftover surplus at the bottom. Keep the $15.82B / $7.5B / 3.1x labels.
- The header says the $2B return is funded from surplus, not the capex budget, but the first row is all about the warehouse's 22.2% after-tax return and the last row says the special dividend is $2.26 per $1B, then $4.51 per $2B. Three different units sit in one table with no column telling the reader which measure each row is proving. That is a table that needs a voice-over. **Fix:** Split into two pages. Page 4a: 'The $2B is surplus, not capex' — fund-source waterfall from $15.82B operating cash flow to $7.5B capex to $6B return capacity. Page 4b: 'Each $1B of return earns 22.2% in capex, 1.88% and $20.76 per share in buyback, $2.26 in dividend' — one comparable column (return per $1B) with its own measure named.
- Two different cover ratios (OCF $15.82B covers the $7.5B capex plan, i.e. 2.1x; opening cash $20.21B covers it 2.7x) are presented back-to-back with a note that 'the denominator changes.' The reader cannot tell which ratio is the claim. The title asserts $15.82B covers $7.5B, then the box switches to opening cash. A committee will challenge this immediately. **Fix:** Pick one. Make the title 'FY2026 operating cash flow of $15.82B covers the $7.5B FY2027 capex plan 2.1x and leaves $9.38B of free cash flow,' lead the visual with the $15.82B vs $7.50B comparison, and move the opening-cash 2.7x figure to a single labeled footnote or delete it.
- The comparison the whole deck rests on — warehouse capex at 22.2% after tax vs buyback at 1.88% vs special dividend at $2.26/share — sits in a table whose largest cell, the orange 'Warehouse capex' row, says 'Book return, not the next dollar: reference only, not the decision metric.' The headline of slide 4 claims a comparison on one basis, but the table itself disclaims the basis and the orange answer. The reader is told the 22.2% is the point and simultaneously that it isn't. **Fix:** Reframe the row as a claim. State in the title and the shaded box: the board is deciding on the next dollar; on the next dollar warehouse capex earns X% while buyback earns 1.88% and the special dividend returns cash at $2.26/share, so buyback and dividend are the priority. If the 22.2% is historical and non-decision, either remove it or put it in the footnote. Do not let a row sit half orange and disclaimed.
- Title says '$13.3B' in the outline and '$13.29B' everywhere in the body — the cover promise and the exhibit must use one number. The stated $13.3B also sits against the filing's 'approximately $13.38' in footnote 2 of slide 2. **Fix:** Standardise on '$13.29B' across slides 1-5 and use '$13.3B' only as a rounded reference if at all. Keep footnote 2 on slide 2 but state 'the company said approximately $13.38' as the parallel disclosure, not as an alternative headline figure.
- The bridge does not foot. 20.21 + 15.82 - 6.43 - 13.29 - 2.61 - 0.85 = 12.85, which reconciles, but the title claims cash 'covers the $13.29B special dividend 1.5x' while the closing balance shown is 12.85 — the 1.5x is opening cash only and the page does not say so; a reader checking the bars will read the coverage as 12.85/13.29. **Fix:** State coverage explicitly in the title and callout: 'Opening cash of $20.21B covers the $13.29B special dividend 1.5x; after all uses the year closes with $12.85B.' Add a coverage footnote: 20.21 / 13.29 = 1.52x, opening balance, before capex and regular dividends.
- The title claims 'At 12.6% growth the buyback returns 14.5%, against a 6.4% cost of equity', while the same page presents a consensus case of 14.5% versus 14.5%.
