The formulas reference named ranges like {ocf_row}, {capex_row}, {rev_row}, {sh_close_row}, {ni_row} that are literal placeholders and were never resolved to actual row references or defined names. This is not a formula that evaluates — it is a template string. As a result Calc!B10, B11, B12, B19, B28, B34, B35, B36, B41, B43, B47, B48, B51-B60 all read #N/A. **Fix:** Replace every placeholder reference with the actual row/named-range reference. Calc!B10 should be =B8+B9; Calc!B11 should be =B7/<prior-year revenue> or 0 for the actuals column; Calc!B43 should be =B42/B41; Calc!B41 should be =B39-B40. Work through rows 10, 11, 12, 19, 28, 34, 35, 36, 41, 43, 47, 48, 51-60 and resolve each placeholder to a real cell or defined name, then confirm no #N/A remains.
- Twelve Outputs cells inherited the #N/A from Calc column B. Outputs!B8 = [=Calc!B10], Outputs!B22 = [=Calc!B34], Outputs!B25 = [=IF(Calc!B59>=0,"Funded from cash and free cash flow","Needs debt or a smaller r] — the Verdict cell is #N/A and its text is truncated mid-word ('smaller r'). The summary tab shows no view at all. **Fix:** Once Calc column B is fixed, re-point and confirm every Outputs cell evaluates. For Outputs!B25, correct the truncated string to full text, e.g. "Needs debt or a smaller return of capital".
- The Checks tab reports 3 of 11 checks not OK and MasterCheck = ERROR. Two checks (Outputs cash ties to Calc, and the sensitivity-corner tie) are #N/A because they reference the broken column B. A file whose own master check reads ERROR cannot be shared — that is the first thing a client or an IC reviewer opens. **Fix:** Resolve the column B errors, then re-run: Checks!B8, B14, C8 and C14 must return numeric differences within tolerance and 'OK'. Confirm Checks!F3 flips to OK cold, on open, with no manual recalculation needed.
- The cash roll-forward does not tie to closing cash — off by $15,330.48m. Calc!B34 = [=B{cash_open_row}+B{fcf_row}+B{returns_row}+B{intinc_row}+B{debt_row}] should equal Calc!B33 plus the year's movements. A $15.3bn break is not rounding; a component is missing or double-counted (likely the capex/FCF sign or the opening-cash carry). Cash is the spine of this model — if it does not roll, nothing downstream is credible. **Fix:** Trace Calc!B34 against B33 + B10 + B28 + B29 + B30 and identify the missing/mis-signed line. Reconcile so Checks row 'Cash roll-forward ties to closing cash' shows difference 0.
- Closing shares do not tie: opening less retired is off by 3.62m shares. Calc!B58 = [=B{sh_open_row}-G{sh_close_row}] and Calc!B41 = [=B{sh_open_row}-B{sh_ret_row}]. Either the buyback share-retirement formula (Calc!G40 = -G26/share_price) uses the wrong price or period, or FY2026 closing shares (443.1m from the cover, dated 2026-09-29) are being run through a buyback that did not exist in FY2026. 3.62m shares at $1,106.21 is ~$4.0bn — the whole authorisation; this is not a rounding artifact. **Fix:** Make Calc!B41 equal B39-B40 exactly and confirm the same identity holds for every forecast column C-G. If the FY2026 column should show zero retirement, set B40=0 and reconcile the check.
- There is no valuation. No WACC build, no terminal value, no exit multiple cross-check, no comps, no net-debt-to-equity bridge, no per-share value on diluted shares. The Outputs tab stops at 'Verdict' = cash funded from operations. For a capital-allocation model that is defensible as scope, but the digest titles it 'Costco capital allocation model' and the Outputs tab has a 'Verdict' cell (Outputs!B25) that reads like a recommendation. If this is the decision support for the special dividend and $4bn buyback, the committee will ask what the capital return is worth versus retaining it — and the model cannot answer. **Fix:** Either (a) add a Valuation tab: WACC built up and sourced (risk-free from F21 5.15%, beta, ERP), mid-year convention, terminal value share of EV shown, exit multiple cross-check, equity bridge (net debt from debt_fy26 $3,914m, cash_fy26 $20,207m), per-share on diluted shares; or (b) if capital allocation only, retitle Outputs!B25 to 'Funding test' and state explicitly on the Cover that no valuation is in scope.
- Interest is computed on opening cash only and at a flat 4.1% for five years. Opening cash is a flow item that changes every year as buybacks and dividends are paid (B11 = 832.65 to F11 = 1,168.37; interest income total $4,037.49). The cash balance that is actually invested (average of opening and closing) is never used, and a 13-week T-bill yield sourced from one date is being applied to a five-year horizon. **Fix:** Change the interest formula to =AVERAGE(B10,B17)*cash_yield (or at minimum AVERAGE of opening and closing) and replace the single cash_yield with a term structure input (e.g. 12-month, 2-year, 5-year yields) added to Inputs; re-run MasterCheck and the interest total.
- OCF margin is a single flat ratio applied to revenue in every year (B6 =B5*ocf_margin; FY2027 $16,773.9m to FY2031 $21,176.66m) and capex grows at a constant 4.0% per year even though the source is a specific FY2026 guide of ~$6.5B with 13 warehouses to open. There is no operating leverage, no working-capital seasonality, and no link between capex and the warehouse-opening schedule. The digest labels these as sourced but there is no stated range, no downside case, and no justification for holding a 5.2% operating margin flat while revenue compounds at 6%. **Fix:** In Inputs, add an explicit ocf_margin range (e.g. low / base / high) and a rationale line; break capex into maintenance capex and growth capex per new warehouse so that the FY2027 $6,500m rollout is driven by the 13-warehouse plan and the annual maintenance number is sourced separately. At a minimum, put a stress test on ocf_margin at 4.5% and 5.5% and show the resulting ca_min_headroom.
- The 'answer' is an artifact of a $5,000m cash floor with no source and no range. Model!B24 = 8,929.02, i.e. the headline 'Yes' survives only because the floor is set below opening cash (20,210). Change the floor to $10,000m and headroom falls; the conclusion is manufactured by an unsourced plug. **Fix:** Source min_cash from Costco's actual liquidity policy or the minimum operating cash disclosed in the 10-K (cite the filing and page), or replace the floor test with a stated minimum-cash range (e.g. low/mid/high) and show Outputs!B17 under each. Do not label a cell 'assumption' and then let it drive the answer.
- A $30 special dividend on 443.1m shares is immediately $13.3B (Model!B15 = -13,293), which is 42% of total cash returned (31,631.81) and burns most of the first-year FCF. It appears stale/incorrect: Costco's actual 2023 special dividend was $15/share, and a $30 declaration at $1,106/share is a 2.7% one-off yield. A committee will stop on this cell. **Fix:** Verify the special dividend amount and date against the primary source (Costco press release / 8-K). If the amount or date differ, update special_dps and rerun; if the figure is correct, add a dated footnote next to the input stating the announcement date, record date and pay date so the number is defensible.
- Free cash flow is revenue x margin, so it grows dollar-for-dollar with revenue and ignores working capital, taxes, lease/operating cash movements and capex seasonality. Check the math: 15.82/303.15 = 5.22%, but the model uses 5.2% and applies it to a revenue line that grows from 324,370.5 to 425,183.56, implying OCF of 22,194.58 by FY2031 with no working-capital seasonality Costco's negative cash-conversion cycle actually creates. This is a growth-rate model dressed up as a cash model. **Fix:** Rebuild row 6 as revenue x net income margin plus D&A less a working-capital change line (separate row), anchored on FY2026 10-K figures; tie beginning and ending working-capital balances. If a margin-only model is kept, disclose it and show FCF sensitivity to a +/-50bp margin change.
- The 6.0% revenue growth is the single biggest driver of the $51.4bn FCF, yet it is sourced to historical growth (7.8%) with no range, no rationale for why it drops to 6.0%, and no linkage to the 16 new warehouses cited. A committee will ask why growth falls from 7.8% to 6.0% and what happens at 5% or 8%. **Fix:** Set rev_growth to a driver-based build (comp sales + new warehouse contribution) or, at minimum, add a range 4.0%-8.0% with rationale and run the sensitivity on Outputs. Cite the 10-K three-year rate and the warehouse count as the basis.
- A flat 5.2% OCF margin for five years is a plug, not a driver. Costco's OCF margin has moved with working capital; holding it flat through 2031 with no range or rationale hides the working-capital assumption. The $51.4bn FCF swings directly with this. **Fix:** Break OCF into net income + D&A +/- working capital, or at minimum state the margin is held at FY2026's 5.2% with a range 4.7%-5.7% and sensitivity. Source the historical range from the 10-K.
- The $30 special dividend costs $13,293m in year one and drives closing cash from $20,210 to $11,429 — a 43% draw. The model does not state how it is funded or whether it breaches the $8,000 floor; headroom at B20 is only $3,429. If the special dividend is $35 or growth is 4%, the floor is breached and the 'Funded without new debt?' answer flips. This is the single most committee-visible assumption and it has no financing narrative. **Fix:** Add a line showing the special dividend is funded from opening cash plus FCF, quantify the minimum headroom ($3,429) and state the maximum special dividend the cash supports. If outside financing is contemplated, show it; if not, say so explicitly.
- Source reads: 'F39 news report Costco declares $30 special dividend and reauthorizes $4B buyback; primary 8-K/press release not located.' The $30 special dividend drives a $13,293m first-year cash use (Model!B15 = -special_dps*B12), which is 28% of total cash returned of $48,247m. A media report is not a source for a board-level capital allocation answer. **Fix:** Locate the 8-K/press release for the $30 special dividend, cite the filing date and accession, or remove the special dividend from the base case and present it as a downside/upside toggle. Do not leave a media reference as the underpinning of a blocking cash use.
- Opening shares are sourced as 443.1M at 2026-09-29, but Model!B19 = B12 - B18 = 443.1 - 3.62 = 439.48 and Model!F19 = 425.02. Over five years the model retires only 3.62m shares a year against a $4,000m annual buyback (Model!B16) at $1,106.21 (Inputs), i.e. 3.62m shares per year, which is arithmetically correct (4,000 / 1,106.21 = 3.616) but materially inconsistent with the source that total share repurchases were $848.0M in FY2026 against a $4,000m authorization. Either the authorization is a ceiling not a run-rate, or the retirement math is understating retirements. Either way the closing share count and the $5.88 dividend per share on average shares (Model!B14) are both overstated versus the source. **Fix:** Reconcile: (i) confirm whether $4,000m is an annual run-rate or an authorization ceiling and state it in the input label; (ii) if annual, the math is right and the source note must say 'assumed annual spend of $4,000m'; (iii) if a ceiling, replace with FY2026 actual repurchases of $848.0m and label the source. Add a check that buyback spend / share price = share retirements, and a check that closing shares = opening - cumulative retirements.
- Revenue growth 6.0% is sourced only to '3-year CAGR 7.8%' and capex growth 10.0% to a 3-year 14.2% CAGR — these are growth rates, not drivers, and 6.0% is a ~180bp step down from the cited 7.8% with no reconciliation. Nothing in the file shows warehouses, comps, membership fee income or gasoline/FX split, which is what actually drives Costco revenue. **Fix:** Replace the single revenue growth input with a driver build on Inputs: new warehouse openings x average revenue per warehouse, plus comp growth (comp sales and membership fee income), tied to the 10-K disclosures; replace capex_growth with a build of new warehouses x cost per warehouse plus remodels, cross-checked to the $6,500m FY2026 guidance in capex_base. Keep one line reconciling the driver output back to the 3-year 7.8% CAGR already in the book.
- The digest shows 'range  to ' empty for rev_growth, ocf_margin, capex_growth, dps_growth, cash_yield, down_growth, down_margin_cut and the rest. A committee cannot see whether 6.0% growth or a 1.0% margin cut is the middle of a plausible band or the low end of one. ca_min_headroom (Model!B24) = 10,620.25 and ca_down_min_headroom (Model!B36) = 6,048.07 both sit above the floor, but with no ranges nobody can tell how close to the floor the low case actually is. **Fix:** Fill the range column on Inputs for every assumption with the low and high values you would defend (e.g. rev_growth low 3.0% / base 6.0% / high 8.0%; ocf_margin low 4.2% / base 5.2% / high 5.5%; capex_growth low 8% / base 10% / high 14%), each with its source. Any assumption with no source and no range should be flagged as a judgment call in a one-line note in the adjacent column.
- Interest convention is inconsistent with the label and with the dividend convention. Model!B11 is labelled 'Interest income (on opening cash: on average cash' — formula is =B10*cash_yield, i.e. opening cash, not average. Model!B14 regular dividends uses AVERAGE(B12,B19) (average shares). Two different conventions in one model, unexplained. **Fix:** Either change B11:F11 to =AVERAGE(B10,B17)*cash_yield to match the average-balance convention used for dividends, or change the label to 'Interest income (on opening cash)' and add a one-line note in Inputs that the convention is opening balance, stated consistently. Do not leave the label half-edited as it is now.
- The cash floor — the single number that determines the headroom result (2,891.94 base, -1,678.42 downside) — is sourced only as 'assumption: computed floor of 2 weeks of revenue = $303,150m x 2/52'. That is a made-up convention, not company policy. No citation, no range, no explanation of why 2 weeks. **Fix:** Replace with a sourced figure: either Costco's stated minimum operating cash (pull from 10-K liquidity discussion) or, if none exists, label it explicitly as 'Analyst assumption: 2 weeks of revenue' with the range tested (1 week to 4 weeks) and show the headroom at each. Currently the reader sees a concrete dollar figure with a hand-wave source.
- Buyback of $800m/yr is sourced to a '$4B share repurchase program reauthorized, expiring January 2030' plus FY2026 actual $848m. The program authorization is a ceiling, not a plan. Using 20% of a $4B authorization as an annual run-rate with no stated rationale is an unsourced assumption dressed as a citation. **Fix:** State the driver: either 'FY2026 actual $848m held flat' or 'management has run ~$800m-$1.0B/yr; we assume $800m'. Give the range ($0 to $848m) and show headroom impact at both ends. If the plan is to preserve cash, say so; if to return it, say so.
- Downside lowest headroom is -$1,137.46 (Outputs!B19) and the answer cell reads 'No: in the downside cash falls below the floor', but no funding plan, no draw on the revolver, and no comparison to the $4,000m min_cash is given beyond the floor breach. A committee cannot approve a capital allocation plan that is unfunded in its own downside. **Fix:** Add a downside funding row to Model and Outputs: show the incremental debt or reduced buyback required to hold closing cash at min_cash ($4,000m), compute the gap as $4,000 - $2,862.54 = $1,137.46m, and state in Outputs!B20 the specific action (e.g. 'Funded by cutting the $4,000m buyback by $1,137m in 2031').
- Two of the three drivers that determine the answer (revenue growth and capex growth) carry ranges but no scenario/sensitivity is run on them in the outputs; the only sensitivity is a 2-way grid on Model rows 39-44 whose formula is truncated and whose link to Outputs is unstated. The 6% growth and 8% capex are midpoint picks, not tested. **Fix:** Wire the Model!B39:F44 grid (growth change vs investment plan change) to a named output and surface it on Outputs, and add one-way sensitivity rows on rev_growth (4.5%/6.0%/7.5%) and capex_growth (5%/8%/12%) showing ca_cash_end and ca_min_headroom for each.
- The written Answer reads 'Over the forecast the business generates $56,423m of free cash flow and returns $47,384m to shareholders; cash ends a...' and stops there — it never states that the downside (Outputs!B19 = -1,137.46, Outputs!B20 = 'No: in the downside cash falls below the floor') fails in FY2031. The central conclusion of a capital allocation model is whether the plan is affordable; the Answer tab is the one thing a committee reads. This is the single most important output and it is missing. **Fix:** Rewrite Outputs!B4 to lead with the base case AND the downside in one sentence: 'Base case: FCF of $56,423m funds $47,384m of cash returned and closing cash of $33,181m, $29,182m above the $4,000m floor. Downside (3% growth, 100bp margin cut, 15% capex overrun): closing cash of $2,863m in FY2031, $1,137m below the floor — the plan is not fundable at the current dividend and buyback without a change to one of the drivers.' Then add a bullet naming which driver to change first (see downside issue).
- The downside cash line reuses the BASE case interest income (B11 across to F11) rather than the interest the downside's own — and rising — cash balance would earn. Check: Model!F33 = 2,862.54, but the closing cash path in the downside is different from base in every year (FY2031 downside closing cash 2,862.54 vs base 33,181.51), so FY2031 downside interest is materially overstated at base-case F11 = 1,103.94. The interest is a formula link to the base interest row, not an independent calc; this hides the fact that the downside is closer to breaking the floor than it looks. It is not an error in the check (the check ties because it is defined that way), but it makes the downside understate the cash shortfall. **Fix:** Replace B33:F33 in the interest component with a downside interest line: down_int = prior-year downside closing cash * cash_yield, and add a row so Outputs!B19 reports ca_down_min_headroom from the re-computed path. Then the printed downside headroom is honest.
- ca_min_headroom = 7,428.75 at Model!B24 and Outputs!B21 says the base-case lowest headroom is FY2027 — but Model!B10 opens at 20,210 and Model!B15 pays the $30 special dividend ($30 * 443.1 = 13,293) at B15. That is intentional (special is 'paid at the start of the first y'), but the reader sees FY2027 as the trough year with 7,428.75 headroom, which happens to be above the 4,000 floor by only 1,857 on a base-case OCF of 16,774. The model then grows headroom monotonically. There is no scenario where the special dividend is smaller, delayed, or paired with the buyback being cut — the special is $30 fixed (Inputs, special_dps range 30 to 30). A source note says 'special cash dividend of $30 per share declared 2026-11-11' — that is a declaration, not a forecast. Locking a range of 30-to-30 into a forecast is a plug. **Fix:** Widen the special dividend range in Inputs from '30 to 30' to at least '0 to 30' with the $30 case cited as the declared amount and the 0 case cited as the alternative (no special; larger buyback). Then add a sensitivity on the special (e.g. 0/15/30) in the Outputs tab, because for the base case it is the largest single line of cash returned after buyback and the one thing the answer hinges on.
- The headline answer at Outputs!B4 reads 'Over the forecast the business generates $56,423m of free cash flow and returns $47,695m to shareholders; cash ends at $32,859m' — this is the base case only, and it does not disclose that the downside case breaches the $4,000m cash floor by -$1,460m in 2031 (Outputs!B19/B22, Model!B36). A committee reading the answer cell alone would conclude the $30 special dividend (Inputs special_dps=30, Model!B15=-$13,293m) and the $4,000m/yr buyback are funded. They are not, in the stress case. **Fix:** Amend Outputs!B4 to append the downside explicitly, e.g. '...cash ends at $32,859m (base); in the downside case cash ends at $2,540m and headroom over the $4,000m floor is -$1,460m in 2031, so the $30 special dividend is not funded in the stress case.' Do not change the numbers; only state both.
- The $30 special dividend (-$13,293m, Model!B15) and the $4,000m/yr buyback (Model!B16=-4,000) are held constant in the downside (Model!B33 = cash + B32 + B11 + B14 + B15 + B16). The downside is not a downside of the capital plan — it is a downside of operations with the same capital plan. A committee will read the -$1,460m headroom (Model!B36) as the consequence of not flexing the return of capital, not of the business deteriorating. **Fix:** Add a second downside column or a toggle by the special dividend that cuts/suspends special_dps to 0 in the downside and reruns Model!B33. Report both: headroom with the plan unchanged and headroom with the special dividend cancelled. This is the number the board needs to see.
- The cash floor is the single assumption that drives the entire 'Funded without new debt?' conclusion (Outputs!B17 = 'Yes' on ca_min_headroom = 10,620.25 vs min_cash 4,000), and its source is literally the word 'assumption'. The $30 special dividend is a hard-coded single point with zero range, and $30 x 443.1m = $13,293m at Model!B15, i.e. 42% of all cash returned (31,917.32). **Fix:** Source min_cash to Costco's stated minimum operating cash / revolver covenant (10-K liquidity discussion) and show the floor as a range 3,000–6,000 with the conclusion sensitivity to it; state the $30 special as an announced event with its filing/8-K reference and add a range or '0 if not declared' toggle, since removing it changes the answer from funded to funded-by-a-hair.
- Three of the four real drivers are labelled 'assumption' or traced to a 3-year historical CAGR (F19 FY2026 three-year capex CAGR 14.2%) rather than to a driver or guidance. Rev growth 6.0% has no tie to compstore sales, membership fee changes, new-club openings or FX; the capex growth 8.0% sits below the 14.2% historical CAGR and below nothing in the material explains the deceleration. **Fix:** Rebuild rev_growth from comp-store sales (10-K disclosure) x new-club unit growth + membership fee step-up, with the FY2027 guidance range as the bound. Replace capex_growth with a maintenance-plus-growth build from club openings and remodels. Each driver gets a filing citation and a stated range, not a single point.
- The downside case never suspends the buyback in any year (ca_down_suspended = 0, lowest downside headroom 7,609.29 > 0), so the 'downside' is not a downside: it does not test the mechanism it was built to test. The downside only flexes revenue growth, margin and capex, and still clears the floor every year even at the low end of the grid (ca_grid_low 31,671.82). **Fix:** Extend the downside grid to where headroom actually goes negative (e.g. down_growth 0%–2% combined with capex_overrun 20% and a T-bill yield cut to 2%), and report the year the buyback suspends in the Outputs. If it still does not suspend, delete the suspends-flag row and say so.
- The sensitivity grid is not built off the model. Row 42 formula reads '=cash+revenue_base*ocf_margin*IF(ABS((1+rev_growth+$A42...' — it recomputes cash from cash + revenue_base*ocf_margin with an IF/ABS switch, not from the Model's own flows (rows 5-17). Grid low/high (B48=31,671.82, B49=58,048.82) therefore do not tie to ca_cash_end (49,999.26) by construction; the centre of the grid cannot be reconciled to the base case on the face of the workbook. This is the single biggest thing a committee will pull. **Fix:** Rebuild rows 42-46 as a proper data table (or INDEX/MATCH on a delta-recalc of rows 5-17) so the centre cell equals ca_cash_end = 49,999.26 and each row/column is the base case flexed by ca_g_step and ca_c_step.
- These three drive 31,917 of 'cash returned' and 5,283 of interest income, and they are the weakest-sourced inputs in the book: special_dps rests on 'F38 Yahoo Finance 2026-11-11' with no filing or company confirmation; buyback = 800 has a stated range of 0 to 1,000 with no stated basis for 800 versus any other point in that range; cash_yield = 4.1% is sourced to a 13-week T-bill (F24) but is applied to all five years flat — the range shown is 2.5% to 4.5% and the model takes the top of the range for the full period. **Fix:** Cite the Costco press release or 8-K for the $30 special dividend (or replace with the disclosed figure); set buyback to the run-rate implied by the $4B authorization divided by its stated useful life, and show that arithmetic in the source cell; split cash_yield into a near-term rate tied to the T-bill and a long-run rate at the lower end of the stated range.
- The downside revenue growth value (3.0%) sits OUTSIDE its own stated range (0.0% to 2.0%). Either the value cell or the range cell is wrong; as written, the downside case is not the stated downside. A committee will catch this on the first read of the assumption book. **Fix:** Reconcile the value and range: either set down_growth to a value inside 0.0%-2.0% or restate the range. Whichever is the intended downside, the other cell must move.
- A $30/share special dividend - a $13,293m cash outflow (Model!B15 = -special_dps*B12) - is the single largest discretionary item in the model and it is sourced to a Yahoo Finance headline with the source note itself admitting no 8-K or press release was located. That is not a board-grade source for a $13B capital decision. **Fix:** Replace the Yahoo citation with the primary document (Costco 8-K press release announcing the special dividend, with the declaration date and record/payment dates) and cite the exhibit/page; if no 8-K exists, set special_dps = 0 and flag it as a scenario, not a base-case input.
- The downside suspends only the buyback, never the regular or special dividend — yet Model!B15 books a $13,293m special dividend at the start of year 1 in the base case and the downside inherits it (Model!B34 = cash+B32+B11+B14+B15+B33). The result is ca_down_min_headroom = 4,928.7 with ca_down_suspended = 0: the downside case never actually bites because it keeps $800m/yr of buyback and the full dividend. A committee will read 'funded in the downside: Yes' and ask what happens if the dividend is the lever. **Fix:** In Model!B34, add the same suspension logic to Model!B15 (special) and Model!B14 (regular), i.e. IF(prior closing cash < min_cash, 0, dividend), and re-run ca_down_min_headroom. Then report the number of years each return line is cut in Outputs!B21, not just buyback suspensions.
- The model calls B6+B7 'Free cash flow' but B6 is operating cash flow plus capex only — the $5,184.81m of interest income (Model!B26) is swept into closing cash (Model!B11) and excluded from FCF, while taxes, working capital and any debt service are absent entirely. There is no equity bridge, no WACC, no terminal value and no per-share value anywhere in the workbook, so 'capital allocation' is a cash sweep, not a valuation. A committee cannot act on it without knowing what the $53.5bn of 'FCF' is worth. **Fix:** Rename Model!B8 to 'Operating cash flow less capex' or add the missing lines (taxes, working capital, interest) so the label matches the arithmetic; and add a returns page — FCF yield on the $1,106.21 share price (Implied FCF yield = 53,489.8/ (443.1 × 1,106.21) = 10.9%) at minimum — so the recommendation is anchored to value, not just to a cash balance.
- This is a capital allocation model with no valuation layer: no WACC build, no DCF, no terminal value, no exit multiple cross-check, no equity bridge, no per-share value. Outputs!B4 concludes 'Over the forecast the business generates $54,873m of free cash flow and returns $31,917m to shareholders; cash ends at ...' — a liquidity arithmetic, not a valuation. An IC cannot act on whether $31.9B of returns is the right use of cash without the value it creates. **Fix:** Add a Valuation tab: unlevered FCF from Model rows 6-8, WACC built up from observed risk-free rate, Costco beta, and the $4.0B min cash as debt-like, terminal value at both a Gordon growth rate and a peer exit multiple (Walmart, Target, BJ's), and an equity bridge to value per diluted share. Cite each input.
- A $13.3B special dividend ($30 × 443.1M shares = $13,293m, Model!B15) is embedded in the base case with range '0 to 30' and sourced to 'F37/F46 special cash dividend of $30 per share ... declared 2026-11-13 and payable 2026-12-04'. If it is declared, it is not an assumption — it is a fact and should be in the opening cash / a fixed line, not an assumption with a range. If it is not declared, the source is wrong. As written, base-case closing cash of $48,555m is $13,293m lower for an event that has either already happened or has not. **Fix:** Move the $30/share special dividend to a fixed input (no range), dated 2026-12-04, and show it as a memo line below the uses. Then run the base case without it and show both — the board needs to see the pre- and post-special cash position, not one blended figure.
- Source reads "assumption" with no range shown (only a stated 4,000-8,000 in the digest, which is a sensitivity, not a source or rationale). Every headline output is a function of this number: ca_min_headroom (Model!B24 = 6,872.87) and the "Funded without new debt? = Yes" on Outputs!B17 flip to No if the floor is set materially higher. **Fix:** Set min_cash from a source: peak weekly operating cash need, minimum operating balance held per the 10-K liquidity note, and debt-covenant/rating-agency minimum cash, then show the 4,000-8,000 range and state which number is the recommendation and why. Add a check that ca_min_headroom and Outputs!B17 react correctly across that range.
- There is no valuation content anywhere in the workbook: no WACC build, no DCF, no terminal value, no equity bridge, no per-share value on diluted shares, and no cross-check between the capital-allocation plan and value creation. The file is titled "Capital Allocation Workbook" but answers only a liquidity question (does cash stay above the floor). **Fix:** Either retitle to a liquidity/capital-return capacity model and remove valuation language, or add the valuation engine: FCFF from the same drivers, WACC built from CAPM with sourced risk-free (the 4.12% 13-week T-bill already in Inputs is not a risk-free rate), beta, ERP and Costco's actual cost of debt; terminal value shown as % of EV with an exit-multiple cross-check; equity bridge (net debt, minorities) to diluted shares (Inputs!shares = 443.1); and a sensitivity of value per share to WACC and terminal growth.
- The special dividend is treated as a fixed FY2027 cash outflow on a Yahoo Finance headline from a single dated day (2026-11-13). There is no Board declaration, no filing reference, no board-date/record-date confirmation, and the $30 is hard-ranged 30 to 30 (no range). It consumes $13.3B of the $53.1B of forecast FCF (ca_fcf_total = 53,113.78) and is the single largest line after capex. **Fix:** Replace the Yahoo source with the declaration source (8-K/press release): declaration date, record date, pay date, per-share amount, and total. If not yet declared, model it as a scenario toggle (declared / not declared) with the undeclared case as the base and the declared case as an upside, and re-run the downside with and without it.
- The single most consequential assumption in the model — the floor that decides the headline 'Funded without new debt? Yes' at Model!B24 = 6,873.27 and drives the downside breach at Model!B40 = -2,653.37 — is recorded with source 'assumption' and a range of $4,000-$8,000, with no rationale, no tie to a peer or policy benchmark, and no chosen-point justification. At the top of that range ($8,000) the base case still clears (headroom $4,873m) but the downside deepens; at $8,000 the entire 'Yes' answer is one input away from flipping. **Fix:** Document the floor in the Inputs tab next to min_cash: state the policy basis (e.g., Costco's own disclosed minimum operating cash, a multiple of working-capital swing, or a stated board policy), pick a point inside the $4,000-$8,000 range, and add a one-line rationale and a citation. If no disclosed policy exists, say so and justify the point chosen against the observed cash trough in the historical 10-K balance sheet (draw from the filing already cited at F1/F5).
- Model!B15 = -13,293 books the $30 special dividend as paid at the start of the first forecast year (FY2027), but the source says payable 2026-12-04, i.e. in the year before the forecast opens. Opening cash at Model!B10 = 20,210 is the F5 10-K FY2026 balance at 2026-08-30, which is before the December payment, so the cash has not yet left — but the forecast is labelling a prior-year event as a forecast-year cash outflow and stacking it on top of the FY2027 regular dividend. Worse, the model then drops the paired new $4B buyback authorization to a $1,115m run rate (Inputs, buyback = 1,115) without reframing the FY2027 cash deployment. The downside treatment at Model!B33 defers one year of special dividend if it breaches the floor, which is the wrong lever for an event already declared. **Fix:** Split the model into a stub period (declared 2026-12-04 special, ~$13.3bn, and any FY2026 close buyback) and the FY2027-FY2031 forecast, then start the forecast from post-special opening cash. If keeping the current framing, retitle Model!B15 to show it as a stub outflow and re-source the paired $4B authorization: the Inputs buyback of $1,115m is the remaining 2023 authorization (F50, expiring January 2027), which is stale relative to the F40 new $4B authorisation — use the new authorisation as the base run rate and justify the level.
- The output is closing cash of $28,886m in FY2031, undiscounted and with no terminal value, exit multiple, WACC or equity bridge. If the purpose is capital allocation versus valuation, fine; if this is being used to argue the shares are worth anything, there is no valuation here at all. The 'Answer' text (Outputs!B4) reads 'the business generates $52,751m of free cash flow and returns $47,384m to shareholders; cash ends a...' — that is a liquidity statement, not a valuation, and it is being presented as the answer. **Fix:** State the question the workbook answers on Outputs!A4 ('Can Costco fund its plan and returns without new debt?' — answer Yes/No), and if a valuation is required, add a separate DCF tab with a built-up WACC, terminal value as a % of EV and a net-debt-to-equity bridge on diluted shares. Do not call $28,886m of closing cash a valuation output.
- The $30 special dividend is a one-off declared 2026-11-13 and payable 2026-12-04, i.e. it has already left (or is contractually leaving) the balance sheet, yet it sits in a FY2027-FY2031 forecast as year-one outflow and drives the entire cash trajectory: without it, closing cash would be ~$42.2bn, not $28.9bn. It is also treated as fully fundable from cash in the base but 'deferred if it breaches the floor' in the downside (Model!B33), which is not a decision a board can make — the dividend is declared. **Fix:** Remove the special dividend from the forecast years (it is a pre-forecast event) or show it as a memo line 'already paid, excluded from forecast' below the bridge, and delete the deferral logic in Model!B33/B39. If it must be modelled, split it as 'declared and payable 2026-12-04' with a clear dated source and no haircut.
- The interest income formula takes AVERAGE of the opening and closing cash balance AFTER the special dividend is deducted at the START of the first year, and the special dividend is paid at the start of year 1, so the opening balance used in the average is already net of the $13.3B outflow. Yet the amount is then re-deducted in the closing cash roll. Confirmed by the check: opening cash 20,210, FCF 10,274, interest 422, regular -2,603, special -13,293, buyback -1,000, closing 14,011. The special dividend reduces the interest base AND reduces closing cash, so the same cash movement is used twice. Fix: pay the special dividend at period close (or apply an explicit intra-year timing note), rebuild the interest formula on opening balance plus half of current-year cash generation, and re-tie Checks!F3 to the new closing cash. **Fix:** Pay the special dividend at period close (or apply an explicit intra-year timing note), rebuild the interest formula on opening balance plus half of current-year cash generation, and re-tie Checks!F3 to the new closing cash.
- There is no mid-year discounting. Cash flows are undiscounted and a multiple is not implied, but the workbook presents a five-year present-value framework without a WACC, an exit multiple, or a share-count bridge to a per-share value. A committee cannot accept a capital-allocation model that answers 'is this funded?' but not 'what is the per-share value of the plan?'. Add a WACC build (risk-free 4.10% from Inputs!B3, equity risk premium, levered beta, tax shield on debt) and a terminal value with an explicit exit multiple, cross-checked against the 1,106.21 share price and the 438.58m closing share count. **Fix:** Add a WACC build (risk-free 4.10% from Inputs!B3, equity risk premium, levered beta, tax shield on debt) and a terminal value with an explicit exit multiple, cross-checked against the 1,106.21 share price and the 438.58m closing share count.
- Share retirement is computed on dollar buyback divided by today's price: '=B16/-share_price' -> 0.904 and '=F16/-share_price' -> 0.904. The buyback is flat at $1,000m for five years but the shares retired are also flat, ignoring the price path. Closing shares 'B19' -> 442.2 and 'B27' -> 438.58 are understated drift: at the 6% growth and the 4.1% cash yield, a constant-dollar repurchase at a rising price retires fewer shares, and the model does not reflect that. **Fix:** Either assume a buyback price that grows with the forecast (e.g. share_price*(1+rev_growth)^n) or, if the intent is a constant dollar repurchase, derate the retirement by the price path: B18 = B16/-share_price and F18 = F16/-(share_price*(1+rev_growth)^4). Re-run and re-state ca_shares_end (currently 438.58) and the regular dividend on average shares.
- Interest income is computed on '=AVERAGE(B10+B15,B10+B15+B8+B14+B16)*cash_yield' -> 422.41 and '=AVERAGE(F10+F15,F10+F15+F8+F14+F16)*cash_yield' -> 1,857.77. The AVERAGE of opening and closing cash is fine as an approximation, but it embeds a circular assumption that the interest itself is earned on a balance that already includes the interest — the two arguments average a start and end state, but the end state is defined after the interest is added. The cheque clears at the MasterCheck level because the closing cash formula 'B17 = B10+B8+B11+B14+B15+B16' includes B11. This is a controlled circularity that works only because the starting balance and the flows are independent of the yield; if the yield is ever driven by the cash balance (or if cash is driven by a rate), it goes circular and the check will not flag it. **Fix:** Break the tie by using opening cash only for the first year's interest or by adding an explicit circularity switch cell on the Inputs tab (Iterative calculation on/off) that the MasterCheck tests. Document the convention in a note on the Cover.
- There is no valuation anywhere in the file. No WACC build, no DCF, no EV-to-equity bridge, no comps, no per-share value, no terminal value. The file computes cash generation and cash uses and calls it capital allocation. That is the cash engine of a capital allocation case, not the case. A committee cannot sign off a recommendation without the value of the $47bn of dividends and buybacks it is being asked to approve, and there is no way to tell whether the $1,106.21 share price at which buybacks are assumed is cheap or expensive relative to intrinsic value. **Fix:** Add a Valuation tab: (1) WACC build sourced line by line — risk-free from the 13-week T-bill yield already in Inputs (4.12%, F24), equity risk premium and beta with sources, Costco's current debt cost from the 10-K; (2) a 5-year unlevered FCF DCF using Model!F8 FCFs with an explicit terminal value, mid-year convention, and terminal value as a % of EV shown on the page; (3) an exit-multiple cross-check at a peer EV/EBITDA range; (4) an EV-to-equity bridge with net debt and any minorities from the FY2026 10-K balance sheet; (5) value per share on the diluted share count (Model!F19 = 425.02m). Then put the resulting value per share next to Inputs!B21 ($1,106.21) so the buyback decision is priced, not assumed.
- A $30 special dividend is hard-wired into the base case at the first forecast year ($13,293m outflow in Model!B15), and the downside defers it (Model!B33). The material treats a one-time, board-discretionary capital return as a committed assumption with no decision logic: no rationale, no comparison to the reauthorization it cites, no IC discussion of whether $13.3B of $20.2B opening cash is even the right call. This is the single largest use in the model after buybacks and it drives the 'lowest headroom' headline number ($5,956.87 at Model!B24). **Fix:** Separate the special dividend into a scenario toggle (0 / 30) on Inputs, label it 'Discretionary special dividend — not committed'. Present base case WITHOUT it, add a sensitivity row 'cash end if $30 special paid'. Remove the sentence in Outputs!B4 that describes it as returned cash, or qualify it.
- Six of the drivers are 'assumption anchored on...' — cash_yield (4.1% pulled from a Yahoo T-bill close on a single date, no maturity matched to the cash balance), min_cash ($5,000m justified by 'anchored on operating cash flow $15.82B' — a floor is not derived from one year's OCF), dps_growth 5.0% (anchored on FY2026 DPS $5.54, no payout policy or dilution bridge), capex_base/special_dps/down_growth all sourced only to 'intention' or 'news'. None has a rationale sentence in the model explaining WHY the chosen point is more likely than the top or bottom of the range. The range on rev_growth 4.0%-7.8% is 380bp wide and no evidence is given for centring on 6.0. **Fix:** For each driver, add a cell with a one-line rationale citing the comp or policy: e.g. min_cash = '2 months of COGS at the run-rate', dps_growth = 'matches 10-yr Costco DPS CAGR of X%', cash_yield = '1yr Treasury matched to average cash duration'. Where the source is a news search or Yahoo close, replace with the filing or a primary Fed/UST source, and state the date.
- There is no cost of capital, discount rate, or WACC anywhere in the workbook. The digest shows only 'Interest earned on cash' as a rate. The only valuation-flavored cross-check is the grid in Model rows 44–48, and that computes a plain cash balance, not a PV or a value per share. The workbook cannot support the recommendation in Outputs!B4 ('generates $54,511m of FCF and returns $34,091m') without a discount rate. **Fix:** Add a 'Cost of capital' input block to Inputs with at least: risk-free rate (the 13-week T-bill yield already cited for cash_yield can anchor the short end; a 10-year Treasury must be sourced separately), equity risk premium, Costco beta, cost of equity, after-tax cost of debt, target D/E, and a resulting WACC. Show the build-up on a new 'WACC' tab or a section in Inputs. Then add a discounted-cash-flow tab with explicit UFCF (OCF less capex), mid-year convention, terminal value both by exit multiple and by perpetuity growth, and the EV→equity bridge (net debt, minorities). If the intention is purely a liquidity/capital-allocation exercise, retitle the Cover and Outputs to say so and remove any language implying valuation.
- The grid formulas multiply revenue times an OCF margin but carry no capex and no interest: e.g. B44 = cash + revenue_base*ocf_margin*IF(ABS((1+rev_growth+$A44... — capex (row 7) is absent entirely. That means the grid's 'closing cash' is cash + operating cash flow, with capex implicitly zero and with no interest income. ca_grid_low = 27,295.85 and ca_grid_high = 54,250.33 are therefore not comparable to the base closing cash of 47,257.57, which is net of ~$8–9.5B/yr of capex. The high end of the grid (54,250.33) exceeds base closing cash even though the grid is supposed to stress the same model. **Fix:** Rebuild each grid cell as a mini run of the Model rows for that growth/capex pair: revenue → OCF → less capex (row 7 logic) → interest income (row 11 logic) → dividends (row 14) → buyback (row 16) → closing cash. Do not report ca_grid_low/ca_grid_high alongside ca_cash_end until they are on the same definition. If the grid is meant to be a first-year sensitivity only, label it 'FY2027 closing cash, $m' in the row header so no one reads it as a five-year cumulative.
- Circular reference: C33 (opening cash) pulls from B35 (closing cash) which itself depends on C30 which depends on C28 which depends on C33. This is the source of the #VALUE! that cascades into Projection!D28:F28, D30:F30, B33:F33, B34:F34, B35:F35, B36:F36, B37:F37, then into Uses of cash!B11:F20, Sensitivity!B5:F18 and Outputs!B7:B31. **Fix:** Break the loop by replacing C33 with the prior-period closing cash computed on an opening-cash-basis line, e.g. C33 = B33 + B27 + B29 + C28 - interest, or add a circularity-buster cell with an Iterative Calculation switch and a Check that convergence holds within tolerance. Do not leave the loop uncontrolled.
- MasterCheck is ERROR because the shares-outstanding roll-forward does not tie to shares retired (difference of -445.98 million, signed). With 443.1m shares outstanding, a 445.98m error is larger than the starting float — the roll-forward is unusable as a control. **Fix:** Trace Uses of cash!B5/B7/B8 against the buyback dollar-to-shares build. The sign flip on the buyback (-B6/1000*shares_per_bn) likely treats buyback spend as negative and reverses shares retired incorrectly; re-derive shares retired = (-buyback_spend)/1000 * shares_per_bn with consistent signs, then recompute B8 = B5 + B7 and confirm the difference is 0 before Shares outstanding at FY2031 (Outputs!B21) is published.
- Shares outstanding at FY2031 reads 0.72m. Costco has 443.1m shares outstanding in Inputs!B4. The row is compounding a per-period delta rather than carrying the closing balance forward, so F5 = E9 and F8 = F5+F7 = 0.72 make the share count collapse. **Fix:** Set C5 = B8, D5 = C8, ... F5 = E8, and F8 = F5 + F7. F8 must equal 443.1m less ~4.0bn/1,106.21 * 0.9 ≈ 439.8m at FY2031; add a check that |F8 - (443.1 - 4000/1106.21*0.9)| < 0.5m.
- The entire Sensitivity grid is broken, and the interest-rate assumption is hard-coded inside the SUMPRODUCT (0.035, 0.045, 0.055, 0.065, 0.075 in Sensitivity!B5:F9, and 0.02/0.04 in C5/D5). Nothing on this tab will recalculate if revenue_growth changes. **Fix:** Rebuild the grid to read revenue_growth from the header row and capex_growth from column A, referencing Projection lines. Fix the upstream circularity first; then confirm B12 ties to cash_fy31 and B18 returns the breakeven growth after the range rebuild.
- Every summary figure on Outputs that feeds the deck — five-year FCF, total cash returned, FY2031 cash, headroom, funding flag, FCF columns — is #VALUE!. The outputs tab cannot be shown. **Fix:** Do not patch; fix Projection!C33 circularity, Uses of cash row-8 roll-forward and the dividend build, then confirm Outputs!B7 = 5-year sum of Projection!B30:F30 equals the sum of components and is positive, and Outputs!B16 (=IF(SUM(Projection!B37:F37)=5,1,0)) returns 1.
- The cover says 'Costco FY2027-FY2031 Capital Allocation Model' (a five-year forecast) but the Model tab runs only four formula columns: r5 F5 =E5*(1+rev_growth) and the last column is F. The digest shows ca_fcf_total = 62,178.45 as 'free cash flow, all years' = SUM(B8:F8), which is four years, not five. Either the title is wrong or a forecast year is missing. **Fix:** Either retitle the cover to 'FY2027-FY2030' or add column G to the Model tab with the FY2031 formulas and extend the SUM/AVERAGE ranges in rows 11, 14, 22, 25, 26 and the grid (B44:F48 -> B44:G48). Do not ship with the title and the columns disagreeing.
- The sensitivity grid does not roll the forecast — it computes a single-period approximation: B44 =cash+revenue_base*ocf_margin*IF(ABS((1+rev_growth+$A44...). That is one year of cash, not the four or five years of closing cash the base and downside cases produce (base B23 = 72,712.09, grid high B51 = 77,530.33, grid low B50 = 50,875.87). The grid is on a different definition from the cases it is supposed to stress. **Fix:** Rebuild B44:F48 to run the same five-year (or four-year) closing-cash formula as row 17 at each (growth, capex-growth) pair, driving it off the named inputs so a change in rev_growth or capex_growth moves the grid. Then re-set B50/B51 before quoting them in Outputs B23:B24.
- The model has no recommendation and no decision framed. It reports 'generates $59,831m FCF, returns $18,572m, cash ends at $69,954m' and a downside where buybacks are suspended 3 years and cash ends at -$7,461m. That is the central fact an IC needs: on the company's own policy (cash floor $6,430m, buyback $848m/yr, dividend growing 5%/yr), the base case piles up cash the company has not decided how to use, while the downside breaks the floor. There is no recommendation on capital allocation, and no tie to the equity/valuation story. For a capital allocation model going to an IC, that is the question it must answer. **Fix:** Write the recommendation at the top of Outputs: 'Base case: $63.5bn of cash above the floor by FY31 — the board must decide now: (a) raise the dividend, (b) raise the buyback, or (c) hold the cash as optionality. The downside case (-$7.5bn cash, buyback suspended 3yrs) argues for holding more cash than the current floor implies, so at minimum the floor should be raised to 1x capex.' Then tie it: the model already shows 'lowest headroom over the floor $20,841m' base vs '-$13,891m' downside, so the spread is $34.7bn of idle capital through the cycle. State that number.
- The special dividend is mathematically deferred in the downside — every B33:F33 evaluates to 0 because the IF() cash test fails — yet the cell is still labelled 'Special dividend deferred, downside'. No outflow is funded in the downside; the $13,293m quoted is a reclassification of an amount that was never in the downside cash flow. **Fix:** Relabel Model!B39 to 'Special dividend not paid in downside (never funded)' and add a footnote to Outputs!B22 stating it. If the intent is that the dividend is deferred but still paid in a later year, model the later payment explicitly.
- The sensitivity grid is computed on a single-year cash formula, not the full 5-year model: Model!B44 =cash+revenue_base*ocf_margin*IF(ABS((1+rev_growth+$A44... — one year of OCF added to opening cash, not the accumulated B8:F8 build that produces the 73,004.3 base. The grid therefore does not tie to ca_cash_end; it understates the range of outcomes and its low/high are not comparable to the base closing cash. **Fix:** Rebuild rows 44-48 so each grid cell runs the same 5-year cash roll-forward as B17:F17 (revenue path, OCF margin, capex, dividends, interest, buyback) with the row/column growth and capex deltas applied, and reconcile the centre cell to ca_cash_end before re-exporting B50/B51.
- Interest is computed on ending cash that already depends on interest (Model!B17 = B10+B8+B11+B14+B15+B16 includes B11). That is a circular reference that LibreOffice resolves by iteration; Excel may flag it and a client will. It also makes the downside IF() tests in B33/B34 depend on a value that includes the very item being tested. **Fix:** Break the circularity: use opening cash for interest (drop the +B11 term), or add an explicit switch/copy-paste-values for the interest line, and add a Checks row showing that interest on the cash balance is non-circular. Document the chosen convention on Cover.
- The grid's 'investment plan change' column variable appears to scale the final-year cash formula, but B43 = -2*ca_c_step = -20.0% while the inputs carry no named cell ca_c_step that matches 'investment plan change'; the column moves -20% to +20% and the underlying driver is opaque. The B50/B51 outputs (ca_grid_low 45,889.73, ca_grid_high 73,259.42) therefore cannot be reconciled without reading the raw formula, and the cell isn't documented. **Fix:** Add ca_c_step (and ca_g_step) as named, sourced inputs on the Inputs tab with explicit units. Rewrite the row 44-48 formula to reference the grid-axis cells only (no embedded constants), and add a Checks cell showing the grid centre equals the base closing cash (Checks! shows 'grid centre = closing cash less interest'; make that the documented cross-check).
- The interest-income formula averages two values that are identical in structure — (opening cash + special dividend) and that same figure plus FCF, dividends and buyback — which is not a real average of period cash balances and is undocumented. It is a smoothed plug, not a driver, and a committee will read it as such; the 'no circularity' comment in the label papers over the fact that the second half of the average already embeds the flows. **Fix:** Replace with an explicit average-cash calculation, e.g. interest = cash_yield * AVERAGE(opening cash, closing cash), and add a one-line note in the Inputs tab stating the convention and that it avoids circularity. If circularity is acceptable in this file, turn it on with a documented iteration setting and a separate check that the interest line converges.
- $5.88 dps held flat for five years (Model row 13 propagates 5.88 every year) is itself a major capital-allocation decision — Costco has raised the regular dividend every year — and it is asserted with no range and no rationale. The entire answer ($68.1B closing cash, $53.9B FCF, $14.4B returned) is sensitive to this assumption. **Fix:** Set a sourced range (e.g. 0% to 8% based on the historical dividend growth in the FY2026 10-K / annual report) and show the base case at a defensible mid-point. Reflect the range in the grid or a sensitivity, and state the rationale in the source column.
- The workbook is empty in substance: every tab is 3x3, there is no Cover tab, no Checks tab, no assumption book, and no calculation tabs. Rule X12=fail ("No checks sheet. Add one row per integrity check and a master flag on the cover sheet"). Nothing in this file answers a capital allocation question. **Fix:** Rebuild to the standard B.banker_workbook structure: add a Cover tab carrying a master check flag, an Inputs tab with every driver in one place, a Calc tab with live formulas only, a Checks tab with one row per integrity test, and an Outputs tab that ties to the deck. Populate with Costco (COST) actuals from the latest 10-K/10-Q before any projection work.
- Zero live formulas in the entire file. With no formulas there is no tie between inputs, calculations and outputs, no recalc on input change, and no audit trail. Any number shown is a typed constant. **Fix:** Rebuild the Calc and Outputs tabs so every output cell is a formula referencing the Inputs tab (no typed constants outside Inputs). Re-run validate_xlsx and clear X12 and the no_formulas failure before resubmitting.
- No assumption book: no named inputs, no units, no ranges, no sources. Every driver that capital allocation turns on — capex, buyback pace, dividend policy, membership fee economics, ROIC, WACC — is unstated and unsourced. **Fix:** Create the assumption book with one row per driver: name, value, unit, low/high range, source (filing page, guidance, consensus, peer set), and rationale. Minimum set: revenue growth, membership fee growth, gross margin, SG&A %, capex, D&A, working capital, tax rate, buyback authorization, dividend per share, share count, WACC inputs.
- No valuation output of any kind: no WACC build, no DCF, no terminal value or its share of EV, no exit multiple cross-check, no equity bridge, no per-share value, no capital-allocation scenarios. There is nothing to test and nothing to recommend against. **Fix:** Build the valuation: sourced WACC build-up, explicit forecast DCF with mid-year convention, terminal value with the % of EV it represents shown on the page, exit multiple cross-check, equity bridge (net debt, minorities, diluted shares), and per-share outputs on diluted count. Show the buyback/dividend/capex scenarios each as its own column with the per-share result.
- Both growth rates — the two assumptions the entire five-year forecast depends on — are sourced only as 'assumption'. Nothing in the workbook earns the 6% revenue CAGR or the 4% capex growth; every dollar of the $59,349.91M FCF and $54,794.41M closing cash traces back to them. **Fix:** Source each one. For rev_growth: anchor to Costco's 10-K historical revenue CAGR and to consensus revenue estimates, cite the figures, and show the implied FY2031 revenue of $405,683M as a growth rate versus the FY2026 base of $303,150M. For capex_growth: tie to the capex plan behind the FY2027 ~$6,500M guidance (distribution/warehouse buildout, e-commerce). If no filing or guidance supports 6% and 4%, cut them to the sourced rate and show the new outputs.
- Costco's cash is in short-duration deposits and cash equivalents, not locked in 10-year notes. A 5.1% yield on the average opening cash balance produces $6,874.85M of interest income over five years — 9.8% of total uses — and it is unearned. The 10-year is also the wrong benchmark for a rolling cash balance. **Fix:** Re-source to a short-rate benchmark (money market / T-bill yield, or the weighted average interest rate actually disclosed in the 10-K interest income note) and cite the figure. Present it as a range (e.g. 3.5%–5.1%) so the committee can see the interest-income line move.
- The grid is a closed-form approximation, not a recalculation of the model. B31 is 'cash + revenue_base*ocf_margin*IF(...)', it ignores capex entirely, ignores the cash floor draw, ignores interest on opening cash, and starts from opening cash rather than carrying the year-by-year build. Compare Model!B23 = 54,794.41 (the model) to the grid: the grid is a different calculator. A sensitivity that does not move the actual model is not a sensitivity; it is a second, unreconciled number. **Fix:** Rebuild the grid as a live recalc of the model: for each (rev_growth, capex_growth) pair, recompute the full Model!B5:F19 stack and return F17 (closing cash, 2031) and MIN(B20:F20) (minimum headroom). Put the model's own base case in the centre cell and reconcile that centre back to Model!B23 so the grid reads 54,794.41 at the base.
- Dividend is computed on opening shares, not on shares net of the year's retirement: B14 = -B13*B12 = -2,605.43 uses opening shares B12 = 443.1, while closing shares B19 = B12-B18 = 442.2. The retirement (0.904m at $1,106.21 = $1,000m) is effectively treated as occurring after the dividend. Over five years the dividend base is overstated by the cumulative retired share count. **Fix:** Charge the dividend on the average or a defined timing basis (state the convention in Inputs) and change B14 to = -B13*AVERAGE(B12,B19) or -B13*B19 if retirement is assumed at the start of the year; apply the same to the special dividend B15. Re-run the model and confirm Model!B25 and ca_min_headroom change.
- This is a capital-allocation / source-and-use projection only. There is no DCF, no WACC build, no terminal value, no exit multiple cross-check, no equity bridge (net debt, minorities), no per-share value on diluted shares. Nothing in the workbook answers what the cash returned is worth to a shareholder or supports the $1,106.21 share price assumption for the buyback. **Fix:** Add a Valuation tab: build unlevered FCF from the Model FCF rows, build WACC line by line (risk-free = the 5.15% 10-year already sourced; equity risk premium, beta, cost of debt, tax rate - all sourced) and compute a DCF with a stated terminal-value method and midpoint convention. Then a separate equity bridge (net debt = cash minus debt; minorities; diluted shares from the 10-K). Show the terminal-value share of EV as a row on that tab.
- cash_yield = 5.1% is sourced to 'F24 US 10-year Treasury yield 5.15% at 2026-11-13'. Two defects: (a) the rate is mis-applied — Costco's interest income is earned on short-duration cash and equivalents, where the 10-year is the wrong benchmark; the correct input is the yield on cash/short-term investments. (b) The stated source (5.15%) does not equal the input (5.1%), a 5bp unexplained haircut. 'US 10-year Treasury yield' is also stale-dated to a single day. This single input drives ca_int_total = 6,641 and lifts the closing cash position. **Fix:** Replace cash_yield with a rate tied to Costco's actual yield on cash and short-term investments, evidenced by the 10-K/10-Q interest income line and the yield on its short-duration portfolio as of the most recent quarter-end. If 5.1% is retained, state explicitly the source, the period, and why the input differs from the quoted source; correct the 5.15% vs 5.1% mismatch.

### Workbook: Senior partner (50)

- The file's own master check reads ERROR, and the CFO-facing verdict cell Outputs!B25 is #N/A. A workbook that tells the reader 'ERROR' on its face cannot leave the building. **Fix:** Fix every error cell listed in the digest (Calc!B10 through Calc!B60), confirm Checks!F3 flips to OK, and only then release. Add a single cell on Cover: 'Model status: OK — all 11 checks pass' driven off Checks!F3.
- Every column-B output is #N/A. The formulas reference '{ocf_row}', '{capex_row}' literals instead of resolved row numbers — the historical column doesn't calculate at all. Outputs!B19 (cumulative FCF), Outputs!B22 (cash at FY2031), Outputs!B23 (lowest cash above minimum) and Outputs!B25 (verdict) are all dead, so the model currently answers nothing. **Fix:** Replace the placeholders in Calc column B with real row references (e.g. Calc!B10 =B8+B9; Calc!B11 =B7/B7-1; Calc!B19 =B17+B18; Calc!B28 =B25+B26+B27; Calc!B34 =B33+B10+B28+B29+B30; Calc!B41 =B39-B40; Calc!B43 =IF(B41=0,0,B42/B41)). Repoint Outputs rows 8, 12-16, 19-26 at the correct Calc cells (e.g. Outputs!B22 should be =Calc!B54, Outputs!B23 =Calc!B59, Outputs!B19 =Calc!B51). Recalculate and confirm Checks!F3 = OK.
- The cash walk doesn't reconcile: the roll-forward misses $15,330.48m against closing cash. That is the whole point of the cash waterfall — if it doesn't tie, the funding conclusion is unsupported. **Fix:** Locate the leak between 'Cash roll-forward' and Calc!G34 and correct it — the likely gap is that the roll-forward omits one of the FCF, capital-return, interest-income or debt-draw legs (Calc!G10/G28/G29/G30). Re-drive Checks!B14 until the difference is zero. Do not paper over with a tolerance.
- The sensitivity grid's base-point corner doesn't equal the base case, off by $19,877.06m. If a client reads the grid as a scenario table and the middle cell doesn't reconcile to the base model, the entire scenarios section loses its credibility. **Fix:** Rebuild Calc!B64:G68 so the cell at 6% growth / $90m per warehouse equals the base-case cash figure in Calc!G34. The 70/80/90/110 values are hardcoded in Calc!B64:G68 and are not linked to capex_per_store — restructure so capex_per_store drives the grid and the 6%/90 corner equals the base cash cell.
- The answer to the decision is a narrative sentence, not a financial summary. A committee cannot see the sources of cash, uses of cash, or the closing position at a glance. **Fix:** Replace the narrative with a 5-column table: Sources of cash (FCF $59,349.91, interest $4,037.49, opening cash $20,210), Uses of cash (capex, dividends, buyback, special dividend), Net change, Closing cash $35,930.95, and Headroom $6,428.75. Keep the sentence as a subtitle if needed.
- The special dividend and buyback are hard-coded as dollar amounts while the regular dividend rolls forward on shares and growth. A board member will ask why the $30 special is a one-time hit and why buybacks do not grow; the model does not show the policy, only the spend. **Fix:** Add rows below the uses section: 'Share buyback as % of FCF' (4,000 / 59,349.91 = 6.7%) and 'Special dividend as % of opening cash' (13,293 / 20,210 = 65.8%). Use these ratios to drive the forecast so the policy is explicit and stress-testable.
- The only sensitivity analysis flexes capex growth against revenue growth. The board's decision turns on the return-of-capital levers — the special dividend now declared, the regular dividend, and the buyback — and none of them move in the grid. A capital allocation model whose decision variables are outside the sensitivity is not answering the question. **Fix:** Rebuild the grid (or add a second grid on the same tab) to flex the three levers that matter: (1) regular dividend growth per year (dps_growth, currently 8.0%), (2) annual buyback (buyback, currently $800m against a $4B authorization), and (3) special dividend per share (special_dps, currently $30). Hold revenue growth and capex at base and show closing cash (ca_cash_end) and lowest headroom (ca_min_headroom) at each corner. Report the corners, not a continuous surface.
- There is one case. No base / upside / downside, no version for 'board cuts the buyback' or 'special dividend is repeated'. With the buyback only $800m per year against a $4B authorization that expires January 2030, the whole allocation story depends on assumptions the file does not stress. **Fix:** Add a scenario switch (Base / Downside / Upside) as a named input and drive every Model row off it via CHOOSE or INDEX. Define at least: Downside = rev_growth 4%, ocf_margin 4.5%, buyback $2,000m; Base = current values; Upside = rev_growth 9%, ocf_margin 5.5%, buyback $800m. Show ca_cash_end, ca_min_headroom and ca_uses_total for all three on the Outputs tab.
- This is a two-way sensitivity table on revenue growth and capex step, not scenarios. The grid only stresses two drivers, only five columns wide (+/- ca_c_step = 20%), and every cell is labelled by a percentage step rather than by the decision the committee is making. There is no downside case, no base case, no upside case with a name and a narrative. A client reading Outputs cannot see 'what happens to the dividend or the buyback in a recession.' **Fix:** Replace the step grid with three named scenarios on the Model tab — Base (rev_growth 6.0%, capex_growth 8.0%), Downside (rev_growth 0-1%, ocf_margin 4.5%, capex flat at $6,500m), Upside (rev_growth 8%, ocf_margin 5.5%) — each a full column showing FCF, cash returned, closing cash and lowest headroom. Keep the two-way grid on a back-up tab. Put a one-line verdict on each scenario in Outputs row 17.
- The only scenario grid (rows 30-35) tests growth and capex changes, but its results are never shown on Outputs. The Outputs tab carries a single base case and one downstream view. The decision the board faces is whether the $30 special dividend plus the $4,000m buyback plus the $7,020m capex plan can all be sustained — the model never presents the answer to that question in any scenario. **Fix:** Add a scenario table to Outputs with three columns: base, 'no special dividend', and 'capex +8% and revenue +2% slower'. For each, show ca_min_headroom, ca_cash_end, ca_uses_total and the 'Funded without new debt?' line. Title it 'Base case funds all three uses; cutting the special dividend is not required at the plan capex' or whatever the numbers actually say.
- A $30/share special dividend costs $13,293m in year 1 (Model!B15) against $20,210m opening cash. It is the largest single line in the uses stack and it rests on a secondary news report. A client committee will stop on this. **Fix:** Obtain the primary 8-K or press release and replace the source. If it cannot be verified, pull special_dps to zero and rerun — do not send a model whose biggest cash use is unverified.
- The stated purpose is a capital allocation decision and Outputs!B17/B20 answer 'Funded without new debt?' / 'Funded in the downside?' purely by whether cash stays above the $4,000m floor (Model!B20 =B17-min_cash, Model!B34 =B33-min_cash). No debt issuance, maturity or leverage constraint is in the model at all, so the 'without new debt' question is answered by construction, not by the model. A committee will see through this immediately. **Fix:** Either (a) add a debt block to Inputs (opening debt, maturities by year, max net debt/EBITDA or interest coverage the board will tolerate) and make Outputs!B17/B20 read from that lever, or (b) re-label Outputs!B17 as 'Stays above the $4,000m cash floor?' and Outputs!B20 as 'Stays above the floor in the downside?' and delete the 'new debt' framing. Do not leave the current wording.
- The downside shows a breach of the cash floor — ca_down_min_headroom = -1,678.42 — yet Outputs row 20 only says 'No: in the downside cash falls below the floor' with no magnitude, no year, and no consequence. The single most decision-relevant number in the model is delivered as a yes/no. **Fix:** Change Outputs!B20 to name the number and the year: 'No: cash falls $1,678m below the floor in FY2027.' Add a row above it 'Downside: year the floor is breached' returning the first year where Model row 34 is negative. Place B19/B20 above the base-case rows so the reader hits the downside first.
- The 'Answer' cell says cash ends around the floor, but the only material downside number is two lines down (B18 = 2,862.54, B19 = -1,137.46) and nothing resolves it. The workbook poses the question 'can Costco fund the special dividend and buybacks?' and then leaves the reader with a Yes in base and a No in downside with no bridge. **Fix:** Add a short 'What this means' block to the Outputs tab immediately under r4: state in one line the base result (Yes, $33,181.5m ending cash, $7,428.75m minimum headroom) and the downside result (No, $2,862.5m ending cash, -$1,137.5m minimum headroom in 2031), then one line on the lever (e.g. 'funding the $30 special dividend in full is what tips the downside through the floor') and one line recommendation. Use only numbers already in the workbook.
- The workbook says the downside fails but does not size the gap or the fix. A reader cannot act on 'No'. **Fix:** Directly under Outputs!B20, add the shortfall ($1,137.5m at the 2031 trough, from ca_down_min_headroom) and one line on what closes it: cutting the special dividend, reducing the $4,000m annual buyback, or trimming capex growth from 8.0%—quantify the smallest change that clears the floor using only inputs already in Inputs.
- The model answers 'is Costco funded?' (Answer: 'Over the forecast the business generates $56,423m of free cash flow and returns $31,917m to shareholders; cash ends at $49,999m'). That is a solvency check, not a capital allocation decision. A board doesn't approve 'we stay funded'; it approves how much to return vs. hold, and whether the buyback should be bigger, smaller or timed. The workbook never frames that choice. **Fix:** Add an Outputs row 3 titled 'Question: how much cash can Costco return FY2027-FY2031 while keeping the floor?' and add a block 'Return levers vs. the base case' showing closing cash under buyback = 0, 800 (base), and the maximum buyback that still keeps ca_min_headroom >= 0. Use only existing Model rows 16/17/20 to compute it. Then re-title Outputs!A4 to 'Base case: $56,423m FCF funds $31,917m of returns; cash ends at $49,999m'.
- The scenario grid does not use the model. It recomputes closing cash from a one-line proxy (revenue_base*ocf_margin plus a cash term) instead of the row 5-17 engine. So the centre of the grid ($48,389m, F43 vs ca_cash_end $48,389.35) only ties by coincidence of the check, and the 5x5 sensitivity ignores capex growth, dividends, buybacks and interest that actually drive the answer. A committee member who looks at one grid cell and then at the base case will find they were built two different ways. **Fix:** Rebuild rows 42-46 so each cell re-runs the row 5-17 engine (or a compact copy of it) with the row growth and column capex-growth overrides feeding rows 5 and 7. Then re-point the Checks tab 'grid centre = closing cash less interest' line to prove the new centre ties to Model!B17.
- The grid formulas do not look like the rest of the model. Every other row builds from revenue, ocf_margin, capex_growth and cash_yield through explicit closing-cash lines; the grid collapses to '=cash+revenue_base*ocf_margin*IF(ABS((1+rev_growth+$A42...' — a one-line shortcut. It cannot be tied to Model!B17, and the IF(ABS( guard is truncated in the digest, so I cannot tell what it is protecting against. A committee member who asks 'does the grid tie to the base case?' will not get an answer from this sheet. **Fix:** Rebuild rows 42-46 to run the same line-by-line engine as rows 5-23 across the scenario axes, or add a tie-out column that shows the centre cell of the grid equals Model!F17 for the base case and label the cell with the difference. If the grid is a legitimate closed-form shortcut, state the algebra in a cell comment above B42 and add a check row to Checks.
- The $30 special dividend (~$13.3B, Inputs special_dps, sourced to the Yahoo piece) is treated as a certainty in the base and downside cases — B15 = -special_dps*B12 = -13,293 and both cases carry it. This is the single largest discretionary outflow in the model and the board's actual decision is whether to declare it. There is no scenario in which the special is smaller, delayed or skipped. Headroom falls to $9,704m at its lowest in the base; that gap is the whole question. **Fix:** Add a third case column, or a toggle cell in Inputs, that runs special_dps at 0, at $15 and at $30, and report closing cash and minimum headroom for each on Outputs. If the model intends to recommend the $30, say so in B4 and show the headroom assumption the recommendation rests on.
- The downside cash-flow formulas force the buyback to stay at -800 in every year (B33 = 800), so 'ca_down_suspended = 0' and 'Funded in the downside? Yes' are hard-wired, not findings. The model cannot test the decision the board faces: whether to suspend the buyback or cut the special dividend. **Fix:** In Model!B33 and F33, change the IF test to use the opening cash of the same year, not the prior closing cash: =IF(B10+B32+B11+B14+B15+B16<min_cash,0,B16). Then add a second downside branch that keeps the buyback and cuts the special dividend, and report both branches on Outputs rows 19-21 with a clear 'buyback suspended in year X' label.
- The grid's column headers are 'investment plan change (column)' with a step ca_c_step, but the formulas only vary revenue growth and an unnamed investment parameter. No scenario in the grid touches the special dividend, the buyback, or the cash floor. Those are the levers the board controls. **Fix:** Replace one grid axis with the special dividend ($0-$30 per share) and one with the buyback ($0-$800m). Keep growth on a third panel. Label every axis with the name of the driver, not 'change'. Report, for each corner, closing cash and whether the buyback is suspended.
- The stated answer is 'the business generates $54,873m of free cash flow and returns $31,917m to shareholders; cash ends at ...'. That is a feature description of the model, not the answer to a capital-allocation decision. The decision a board or IC is asking is: given this cash, what is the right use of it, and can we sustain the current return policy? The model never frames that question, never ranks uses, and never states a recommendation. **Fix:** Rewrite Outputs!B4 as a single decision sentence, e.g. 'Costco generates $54,873m of free cash flow over FY27-FY31; the current policy (regular dividend + $30/share special + $800m/year buyback) returns $31,917m and leaves $48,555m of cash, i.e. the policy is affordable with wide margin in the base case; the binding constraint is the downside case.' Then add on the Outputs tab a 3-row 'decision summary': the cash generated, the uses, and the excess cash available for a new use (buyback step-up, special, or debt paydown).
- The downside is so mild it can never produce a 'no'. Revenue still grows 2% (vs 6% base) to $334.7B, OCF margin falls only 1pp, capex only overruns 15%. Result: ca_down_min_headroom = 5,514 and ca_down_suspended = 0 and Outputs!B20 says 'Yes' (funded). A committee will read that and ask what the point of the downside tab is. The scenario also does not test the actual risks Costco faces (membership renewal softening, a deflation cycle in gasoline and food, a large step-up buyback or debt-funded special dividend), so it does not discriminate between policy options. **Fix:** Add a true stress case alongside the current downside: revenue flat or -2% for the forecast, OCF margin -2pp (down_margin_cut range already allows 2.0%), capex +25% (capex_overrun range already allows 25%), and a new input 'Downside: buyback step-up to $m' set to $3,000. Report ca_down_min_headroom and ca_down_suspended for it. If headroom still clears the $4,000m floor, say so explicitly in Outputs!B20; if not, show which lever (special dividend, buyback, capex deferral) restores it.
- The downside keeping test omits the new $4B revolver. Assumption book: special dividend 'payable 2026-12-04, paired with new $4B bu[revolver]'. Formula r33 =IF(cash+B32+B11+B14+B15<min_cash,0,B15) tests cash against the floor with no facility line, so ca_down_special_deferred=13,293 and ca_down_cash_end=3,346.63 are computed as if no borrowing exists. If the revolver funds it, the $13.3B special dividend is not deferred — it is debt-funded, and the downside answer changes. **Fix:** Add an explicit new-debt line and a ceiling input (new $4B authorization) to Inputs, route it through Model!r17/r35, and re-run the r33/r34 test against cash plus available facility. Report both cases separately on Outputs: 'special dividend deferred' vs 'special dividend funded by revolver', with closing cash and net debt for each.
- The downside says 'Yes' on a $492m cushion against a $6,000m floor — 8% of the floor. One more bad quarter and it is 'No'. As a committee read, that headline will be challenged, and the workbook has no interval to defend it. **Fix:** Relabel Outputs!B20 and Model!B40's companion label as 'Funded in the downside? (thin — $492m, 8% of the $6,000m floor)'. Then add a third headline scenario in Inputs labelled 'Severe downside' with down_growth=0.0% and down_margin_cut=2.0% (the conservative ends of the two stated ranges) and push Outputs!B18-B22 to that case as well; if headroom goes negative there, say so in Outputs!B4's answer string.
- The workbook proves affordability, not choice. Outputs!B4 says 'Over the forecast the business generates $59,350m of free cash flow and returns $47,666m to shareholders; cash ends at $35,840m' - that is a cash-tie, not a capital allocation decision. There is no page that ranks the uses of cash (buyback vs special dividend vs capex acceleration vs debt paydown) or shows what the board gives up by choosing this mix. A capital allocation workbook that cannot compare two allocations is not answering the question on page one. **Fix:** Add an 'Allocation options' block on Outputs (or a new tab before Outputs) with 3 side-by-side cases - (A) current plan: $30 special + $4B/yr buyback; (B) no special, buyback raised to $7B/yr; (C) no special, buyback held at $4B, cash retained - each showing 5-year FCF, total cash returned, ending cash, ending shares, lowest headroom. Keep the existing base/downside as the 'can we afford A' test.
- Interest income is earned on average cash that already includes the special dividend outflow in the same year (B15).
