The special dividend is paid at the start of year 1 (per the label in row 15), so it should not sit in the average balance earning 4.1%. This quietly overstates interest income and closing cash. Total interest is ca_int_total = 3,946.39 across five years; some of that is on money the company no longer has. **Fix:** Rebuild row 11 so the special dividend leaves the opening balance in the year it is paid: interest = cash_yield * AVERAGE(B10+B15, B10+B15+B8+B14+B16) should become interest = cash_yield * AVERAGE(B10+B15, B10+B15+B8+B14+B16). If B15 is already excluded from the opening balance, state that in the row 15 label; if it is not, move it out of the average and re-tie row 17. Re-run MasterCheck.
- The downside only stresses revenue growth (2.0%), OCF margin (-1.0pp) and capex (+15%). It does not stress the two discretionary items the board is deciding on: the $30 special dividend and the $4B annual buyback. Outputs!B20 says 'Yes' funded in the downside but that is only because Model!B38 shows only 1 year of buyback suspension and Model!B39 shows $0 special dividend deferred — a mild outcome, not a real downside. **Fix:** Add a second downside (call it 'Severe') in Model!B31:B40 that also cuts rev_growth to 0% and holds capex growth at 14.2% (the 3-yr actual, per F19 10-K), and reports suspended buyback years and any special-dividend deferral explicitly on Outputs. The answer to the board is 'can we afford the special under stress', not 'do we survive a 2% growth year'.
- Circular reference / broken chain. B33 = cash_fy26 is fine, but C33 = B35 and D33 = C35 etc. reference closing cash in the same row block, and B35 = B33+B30+B34 pulls in B34 which itself is #VALUE!, so every column C-F propagates #VALUE! down the entire cash waterfall (rows 30, 33, 34, 35, 36, 37). **Fix:** Break the cycle. Make opening cash row 33 = prior-year closing cash from the prior column (e.g., C33 = B35, D33 = C35), and change B28 (interest earned) to reference the OPENING cash cell for that column, not C33/D33/E33 (currently B28 = cash_fy26*cash_rate is correct but C28 =D33*cash_rate pulls closing cash from the prior period — that is a 1-period lag error). Wire it so opening cash → interest → FCF → closing cash, no back-reference.
- =regular_dps*(1+dps_growth)^(B$4-2027) returns #VALUE! in every column. The header row 4 (year labels) is almost certainly text, not numeric, so B$4-2027 is a text-minus-number error. This single error kills rows 11, 12, 13, 17, 18, 19, 20 in Uses of cash and propagates to Projection!B34:F34 and Outputs!B10:B13, B29. **Fix:** Ensure row 4 contains integer years (2027, 2028, 2029, 2030, 2031) as numbers, not formatted text. Then verify B11 = 5.88, C11 = 6.17, D11 = 6.48, E11 = 6.81, F11 = 7.15 (5% growth compounding from 5.88).
- All 25 grid cells return #VALUE!. The SUMPRODUCT uses ROW(INDIRECT("1:5")) and references cash_fy26, revenue_fy26, gross_margin etc., one of which is downstream of the broken cash chain. The whole sensitivity table is dead. **Fix:** Only after Projection rows 30-35 compute, re-check the sensitivity. The SUMPRODUCT is also fragile — replace it with a 5-row helper block (year 1-5 revenue, gross profit, SG&A, tax, FCF) and sum it. That way a client can audit the sensitivity, not trust a black-box SUMPRODUCT.
- Master status = ERROR. Two named checks fail: shares outstanding roll-forward (difference -445.98) and capex intensity FY2027 vs FY2026 (difference 1pp). **Fix:** Shares: Uses of cash!B8 = B5+B7 shows 443.82, but somewhere the roll-forward is off by ~446m shares — likely a units error (millions vs. shares). Trace Uses of cash rows 5-8 against Inputs!B? (shares outstanding 443.1). Capex intensity: Projection!B25 uses capex_growth on the FY2026 plan of -6,500 but the check compares to FY2026 actual capex intensity 2.12% of 303,150; the 1pp tolerance is being breached because the forecast capex is -6,760 (4% growth) over revenue 317,701 = 2.13%, which is fine — so the check threshold or the reference year is wrong. Fix the check logic.
- The downside model has the two most discretionary uses of cash — the special dividend and the buyback — auto-protected by formula. B33 =IF(cash+B32+B11+B14+B15<min_cash,0,B15); B34 =IF(cash+B32+B11+B14+B33+B16<min_cash,0,B16). This means the downside is defined as 'base business stress with automatic payout protection,' which is not the downside the committee will test. In the real downside the board could cut the special, defer it, or cut it further than the formula allows. The model as written cannot answer the question 'what does the downside look like if the board keeps the regular dividend and cuts everything else?' or 'if it keeps the buyback and cuts the special?' **Fix:** Split Model rows 33-34 into three labelled scenarios: (a) 'downside, keep all payouts' (B33=B15, B34=B16, no protection), (b) 'downside, defer the special' (B33=0, B34=B16), (c) 'downside, defer the special and suspend the buyback' (B33=0, B34=0). Put the closing cash and lowest headroom for each in Outputs r18-r22 alongside the current single downside. Label row 34 in the base case as 'mandatory minimum buyback, board discretion above'.
- The downside is a single scenario with one set of numbers. There is no probability attached, no historical or peer basis cited, and no indication whether -5% revenue is a recession, a consumer slowdown, or a competitive shock. A committee will ask 'how bad is this, how likely, and when does it bite?' The workbook cannot answer because the three downside drivers are labeled 'assumption' with no justification. **Fix:** Add an Inputs block 'Downside cases' with three labeled columns — 'Mild recession', 'Severe recession', 'Recession + tariff shock' — each with growth, margin cut and capex overrun, and one line each of source/rationale. The recommended-downside column should be stated up front on Outputs.
- The core capital-allocation decision — how much to return — is not modelled. Buyback is zero and capex is a plug at 7,500 flat, so the only thing the model actually decides is how large the cash balance grows. Outputs!B4 reports '$57,030m of free cash flow and returns $13,027m to shareholders' — 77% of FCF is unexplained. An investment committee reads that as the model avoiding the question. **Fix:** Add an explicit capital-return plan: set buyback to a stated annual figure (even one figure the board is debating, e.g. the $283m currently the upper range bound), and add a line 'Surplus cash held vs returned' so the reader can see what is being recommended. If the decision is 'hold cash', that is the answer the model must state and defend on Outputs, not left blank.
- Three of the four growth drivers are set to zero. A flat-dividend, flat-capex, zero-growth-downside model is not a scenario, it is a base case with the downside dial turned to 'no change' — the downside result (36,792 closing cash, 2,528 headroom) is then a mechanical artefact of one margin cut, not a stress test a board would recognise. **Fix:** Give each driver a defensible value from the filings already cited. DPS growth should track the dividend history in the F42/F54 source; capex growth should reflect the store-opening plan in F39. In the downside, down_growth = 0.0% while base rev_growth = 6.0% leaves a 6pp swing unexplained — either set it to a recession case (the range says -3.0% to 2.0%) or state on Inputs why flat is the downside.
- Your downside is a revenue contraction of -3% with a 1pp margin cut and 15% capex overrun, and it still shows 'Funded in the downside? Yes' with $11.3B of headroom (Outputs!B19) and zero buyback suspension (Outputs!B21). That is not a downside, that is a mild wobble. No committee believes Costco's worst case is -3% revenue when the model's own input range for down_growth runs to -5.0%. The scenario the board would actually lose sleep over is flat-to-negative comps plus the dividend commitment and the buyback still running. **Fix:** Rebuild the downside on the decision's real question: set Inputs!down_growth to the bottom of its own stated range (-5.0%), and add a second stress where the buyback is held at a policy level (say the historical run-rate inside the 0-283 range) rather than zero so the model has to show whether the buyback gets cut. Label Outputs!B20 with the specific assumptions that produce the 'Yes' so the reader knows what has to be true for it to hold.
- Shares outstanding never move: opening 443.1, closing 443.1 (Model!B19), and the all-years closing shares output is 443.1. That is only true because Inputs!buyback = 0. A capital allocation model whose answer to 'what happens to the capital you return' is a share count that never changes is a plug, not a driver. If the buyback input moves off zero, dividends are computed on average shares (Model r14) and the share retiral (r18) only changes the count by the buyback dollars divided by price — that logic is fine, but with buyback pinned at zero the reader cannot see it work. **Fix:** Run at least one case at a non-zero buyback inside the stated range (Inputs!buyback range 0 to 283) and show the closing share count and the average-shares dividend mechanic moving. If buyback is genuinely policy zero, say so on Outputs!B16 with a one-line note ('no buyback assumed; shares flat'), otherwise the 443.1 reads as an error.
- The workbook is empty. Three tabs of 3x3 cells with no content means the model does not exist; nothing here answers any capital allocation question, and the digest confirms 'no calculation tabs', 'no formulas', 'no assumption book'. **Fix:** Rebuild the file from the B.banker_workbook structure: a Cover tab with the decision and master check flag, an Inputs/Assumptions tab with one row per driver (name, value, unit, range, source), driver-based Calc tabs (one per business question), and an Outputs tab that leads with the answer. Populate every cell; an empty stub is not a draft.
- The workbook has no live formulas (validate_xlsx X12 / no_formulas). Without formulas there are no drivers, no linkage from inputs to outputs, and no way for a client to follow the logic — every number is absent or a hard-coded plug. **Fix:** Build the calculation chain: every output must trace to Inputs through live formulas with no hard-coded numbers outside the input tabs. If any figure cannot be derived, put it on Inputs with a source. Nothing downstream of a plug.
- There are no named results and no computed values. The decision-maker has nothing to read — the answer is not on page one because there is no page one. **Fix:** Create named cells for the key outputs (e.g. incremental EBITDA, ROIC, payback, NPV by scenario, capital freed) and put them at the top of the Outputs/cover, each with a one-line title that states the claim, not the topic.
- No assumption book. A capital allocation model lives or dies on its drivers (capex per new warehouse, sales per warehouse, build cost, ramp, WACC, tax), and none are documented with a value, unit, range or source. **Fix:** Add an Assumptions tab: one row per driver with name, value, unit, low/high range and a source (10-K, earnings call, internal). Every downstream number must reference these cells. No source, no assumption.
- No integrity checks and no master flag on a cover sheet. A committee cannot trust a model with no balance, tie-out or reasonableness checks. **Fix:** Add a Checks tab with one row per check (inputs reconcile to source, outputs tie to Calc, balance identity holds, no hard-codes outside Inputs) and a master PASS/FAIL cell on the cover referencing them.
- The special dividend is paid only in FY2027 while the regular dividend compounds. The two most likely client-facing alternatives — a $30/share special in FY2027 versus spreading the $13.3B across the window, or a larger special in one year — are not reconciled, so the headline 'Cash returned' number is a one-off event dressed as a capital allocation policy. **Fix:** Rebuild row 15 as a driver: Special dividend per share (input) times a year-of-payment selector, and add a side-by-side 'Special in FY2027 vs. spread FY2027–FY2029' tie-out on Outputs. Show the cumulative return per share in each case.
- The data table varies growth and capex in 10% (cash) and 10% (growth) increments. These are not the two variables the decision turns on. The decision turns on (1) the size of the special dividend, (2) the pace of buyback against the $4B authorized, and (3) whether OCF margin holds at 5.2% (it was 5.2% in FY2026, and a 50bp miss is a $1.5B annual swing). Growth and capex are second-order for a balance-sheet question. **Fix:** Keep one growth × capex sensitivity as back-up, and make the front grid: special dividend per share (rows: $0, $15, $30, $45) × annual buyback (columns: $800m, $2B, $4B) with the output cell being lowest headroom over the floor (ca_min_headroom). Relabel A30/A31 etc. to match so the axis labels say what they vary.
- The first thing anyone reads is a one-word answer with no quantification of the cushion. 'Yes' hides that headroom is $6.8B, i.e. 0.9x the cash floor — a user cannot see whether that is comfortable or tight. **Fix:** Change Outputs!B9 to a quantified sentence: 'Yes — lowest headroom $6.8B (0.9x the $8.0B floor) in FY2027; cash rises to $54.8B by FY2031.' Put the sensitivity grid directly under it so the reader sees what would flip 'Yes' to 'No'.
- The Outputs tab is a 9-row afterthought: six named results stacked in a column and one Yes/No. There is no answer up front, no page that says what the decision is. The coversheet says 'Capital Allocation Model FY2027-FY2031' and nothing more. A CEO cannot find the point. **Fix:** Rebuild Outputs around the decision: page one states the question ('How much cash can Costco return without breaching the board's $5B floor?') and the answer in one line — e.g. 'Costco generates $56.4B FCF over FY2027-31; returning $20.2B leaves $66.0B closing cash and $22.9B lowest headroom; funded without new debt.' Then one small table of the six named results, one chart of closing cash versus the $5B floor by year.
- The scenarios are a 5x5 grid on the two growth rates (-2% to +2% on revenue growth, -20% to +20% on capex growth) — a sensitivity tornado with no decision attached. The decision turns on how much cash to return and whether to fund a special dividend, and the scenario set does not vary the two levers the board actually controls: buyback and special dividend. Worse, the source note on Inputs!special_dps records a $30 special dividend declared 2026-11-11, yet the input is 0 and no scenario tests it. You have a live board decision sitting in your source notes and you modelled it as zero. **Fix:** Replace the growth-rate grid with three named cases the board will recognise, each varying the controllable levers: Base (buyback $1,000/yr, special $0), Accelerated return (buyback $2,000/yr, special $30/share in FY2027), Stress (rev_growth 3%, buyback $2,000/yr, special $30/share). Report for each: closing cash, lowest headroom vs the $5B floor, and the headroom test. Keep the growth/capex grid as a back-up sensitivity, not the front page.
- 'Free cash flow' is defined as operating cash flow plus capital expenditure and nothing else. There is no working capital movement, no cash taxes shown separately, no interest received inside it — interest is added one row below as a separate line. A client reading 'Free cash flow, all years = $54,872.86m' will reconcile that to the 10-K definition and not get there. You are labeling a memo line as FCF. **Fix:** Either rename row 8 to 'Operating cash flow less capex' and keep the total as-is, or build the full bridge: OCF less capex less working capital movement less cash taxes plus interest received. If you take the second route, add the two missing drivers (working capital % of revenue, cash tax rate) to Inputs with sources.
- The sensitivity grid flexes revenue growth and capex growth and nothing else. The decision that turns on this model is capital return: the special dividend (~$13.3B at B15) and the $800m annual buyback. Neither is flexed. A three-by-six grid of revenue scenarios tells the board nothing about whether they can afford the special dividend if the buyback accelerates or the special is doubled. **Fix:** Rebuild the grid on the two variables the decision turns on: special dividend per share (x-axis, $0 / $30 / $50) against buyback per year (y-axis, $800m / $2,000m / $4,000m). Report closing cash and minimum headroom in each cell. Keep revenue growth as a separate one-way sensitivity.
